# Performance playbook for porting Java libraries to Flint

This is the master record of what makes ported Spring, Spring Boot and other Java code fast, small and quick to build in Flint, and of what made it slow. Every rule comes from a measured finding. Read it before porting a module, and check a finished port against the checklist at the end.

**Keeping it current.** Any commit that improves or regresses build time, binary size or runtime adds or updates an entry here in the same commit. An entry carries:
- the rule;
- the evidence: numbers, commit and how they were measured;
- what enforces it: the gate, test or budget, or "not enforced yet".

A rule that turns out wrong is corrected, not deleted, with the evidence that changed it. Certified numbers come only from proof-harness's measurement queue (`docs/spring/phase1/coordination.md`, "Measurement"); local numbers are labelled as such.

**Why this exists.** Flint's own engines are faster than Go: `bench-rest` serves 1.68× Go's throughput in about 12 MB. A faithful port of Java's design was not: the first certified gs-rest-service run had throughput at 0.75 of Go and p99 at 2.45×. Java code is written for a JIT with escape analysis and a generational collector. It allocates freely, uses reflection-shaped generics and dispatches virtually everywhere, and in D each of those habits has a price. Phase 1 found the prices one by one; this file keeps them, so later ports pay none of them.

## 1. Runtime: CPU, latency and memory

The largest costs are per-request allocation and the collector pauses it causes. On gs-rest-service at 31 allocations per request, druntime ran about 90 stop-the-world collections a second: 7–11% of wall time paused, the GC 39% of user CPU, and those pauses drove p99 (`docs/spring/phase1/gc.md`).

| ID | Rule | Evidence | Enforced by |
|---|---|---|---|
| R-1 | **Engine and framework memory never reaches the GC heap per request.** Only objects user code sees are GC memory: DTOs, entities, strings it receives. Everything else lives in the request arena, on the stack, or in reused buffers (ADR-030 §4). The target for `/greeting` is the user's `Greeting` and its string, nothing else. | /greeting 50 → 16 GC allocations per request across the commits below, with worker instructions 71.1k → 65.5k (web-http, callgrind, LDC). On venus, the DMD and LDC Period tests verify 0 GC bytes to parse an eight-character ISO period and at most 16 GC bytes to render it. Parsing keeps field offsets and integers on the stack, and rendering copies the finished stack buffer once. The directed date difference uses existing Gregorian epoch-day arithmetic and compiles as `pure nothrow @nogc` on both compilers. Duration checks signed millisecond and nanosecond limits before scaling seconds. The DMD and LDC millisecond converters match Java 21 on 4,112 inputs, including negative truncation and both overflow thresholds, with zero allocations on successful boundary probes. Both compilers verify zero GC bytes on a successful negative fractional conversion. | Allocation budget gate, work-queue item 28 (`policy/request-allocations.json`): every request of the three Phase 1 apps, on DMD and LDC in CI (`53be733d`, `e3effb9e`). Period allocation and date-difference tests in `framework/java-time/tests/period_test.d`. |
| R-2 | **Parse once and cache what Java parses on every call:** media types (Spring's `MimeTypeUtils` LRU cache), Accept headers, lookup paths, content types. Never build a `ContentNegotiationManager` or a media-type list per request. | `6ae93070` (Accept in one allocation), `6a48c768` (lookup path in one object; a match without variables allocates nothing), `71216ac7` (content type kept). Content negotiation was 21% of user CPU and 10 GC points before. | Allocation gate |
| R-3 | **Store small collections inline.** Request attributes, parameters, headers and response headers keep their first N entries inside the owning object. `Any` stores small structs (up to 32 bytes) inline instead of boxing. | `27e8d10b`: 8 inline attribute slots, 4 parameter slots, 4 header entries plus 4 single values. `Any` inline (framework-core): a plain struct of at most 32 bytes (no postblit, copy constructor or destructor) lives in `Any`'s own value fields, placed so every pointer word lands on a word the GC treats as a pointer; `Any` stays 56 bytes. gs-rest-service's allocation gate: every handled request −1 (default `/greeting` 19 → 18, 4,784 → 4,736 B; the `Box!Greeting`); Jackson's `LocalDate` and `Nullable!int` property values, likewise. Descriptor field and property accessors (`fieldGet`, `fieldSet`, `getterGet`, `setterSet`) reach the held struct through `Any.structPointer!T`, one type check for inline or boxed, instead of `holds`, `isNull` and `get`: an LDC `-O3` callgrind microbenchmark of `Any.of(Greeting)`, a copy and two property reads went 201 → 149 instructions per iteration (413 before inline `Any`); no allocation change. | Allocation gate; engine-aot `sys/inline` tests, `any_inline_test` and the `structPointer` cases in `aot_runtime` (inline, boxed, null, wrong type, copies, GC lifetime, DIP1000 escape) |
| R-4 | **Create per-handler objects once at startup and share them, but only if they are immutable after warm-up.** Spring creates some per request, such as `ServletInvocableHandlerMethod`. A shared object is never written during a request; a concurrent multi-thread test proves it. | `73d9ecbf` (−1 allocation). | Its concurrency test |
| R-5 | **Pass handler arguments in scoped stack storage.** Use DIP1000 `scope` slices, not `Any[]` arrays or wrapper objects. Fall back to the heap past a fixed count (8). | flint-aot's `d617083c` (`invokeArguments`) plus `73d9ecbf` (−2). | A compile-fail test that stores the slice |
| R-6 | **Recycle engine-internal objects, never user-visible ones.** JSON generators, parsers and buffers come from a bounded compare-and-swap pool and forget their sink and context on release. Never recycle an object user code can still hold: in D, a reference held across threads can tear a slice, which a Java reference cannot. A released instance fails loudly on use. | `852b8a8e`: Jackson per write 576 B in 4 allocations → 0. `c812b1b7`: reads down to the user's objects, e.g. OwnerFieldsDto 1,152 → 176 B. The dates-in-place commit after `1ee6ac1d`: a generator used after `close()` throws `StreamWriteException`; a closed parser returns `NOT_AVAILABLE`. Only the mapper's internal paths recycle, never `createGenerator`/`createParser`, and a failed instance is dropped (its exception keeps it for `getProcessor()`). | `write_allocations_test`, including its use-after-close test |
| R-7 | **No locks or monitors on hot lookups.** Configuration and pattern caches are read without a monitor; publish them immutably. | `cab192b6`: `MapperConfig.bean()` took a monitor that every reactor thread contended on. Hibernate's `PatternCache` likewise. SLF4J's `isDebugEnabled` took the logger factory's monitor and walked a `Level[string]` table up the logger's name on every check; each logger now caches its effective level with the configuration generation it came from, in one atomically read word (Logback's `effectiveLevelInt`). One check went from 2,378 to 30 instructions (callgrind, petclinic-rest error-validation request, LDC release, local); web-http measured 7.3k plus 4.6k per request on petclinic's owner GET. | slf4j-api `slf4j_test` (a level change, the root's included, invalidates every logger's cached level); not enforced by a budget yet |
| R-8 | **Never copy a payload twice.** Stream serialisation straight into the response; don't build a string and then convert it to bytes. | `b7c8891d` (`writeValueAsBytes`), then streaming through recycled generators in `27e8d10b`. | Allocation gate |
| R-9 | **Keep GC roots small and grow them lazily.** Register large engine tables as roots in chunks as they are first used, not all at startup. Size preallocated pools by the configured connection limit. | `8815f2d2`: 8,192 connection slots (4.8 MB) became doubling chunks; the pause per collection fell from 0.96 to 0.64–0.79 ms locally. `9aa14e28`: io_uring rings capped, peak RSS 24.5 → 21.5 MB. Certified RSS 1.82 → 1.61 of Go (`b231d2c2`). | gc.md measurements |
| R-10 | **Wake waiting fibers on completion; never poll.** A level-triggered watch whose callback only records readiness is worse than a poll: until the fiber runs, every loop pass reports the descriptor again, so the loop spins. | `e5839c8`: delayed-peer CPU fell 15× and 12×, and the median reply fell from 1,016 µs to 12 µs. engine-sql's libpq waits (2026-11-24): the PostgreSQL socket watch set a flag and the fiber slept to a 1 ms deadline, so every database round trip cost up to 1 ms of loop spinning. petclinic-rest GET /owners/1, local release build, 2 workers, serial client: 5.1 → 0.10 ms of server CPU and 5.2 → 0.25 ms wall per request; 7,065 req/s at one connection. | `98c4558d` counts fiber resumptions; engine-sql `offload` test: 200 libpq round trips on a reactor fiber in under 100 ms (fails on the polling wait) |
| R-11 | **Control-flow exceptions don't capture backtraces.** Engines return `Result`; generated dispatch converts to an exception only at the user boundary. 404 and validation paths have their own p99 runs. | ADR-030 §3. | The error-path p99 entries |
| R-12 | **Measure system calls before optimising them.** io_uring steps 1–3 (single enter, cooperative and deferred task running) left enters per request unchanged, and the certified runs showed no throughput gain from them. The allocation cuts moved the numbers. | `b231d2c2`, `345d0290`. | — |
| R-13 | **Don't change Spring's public extension-point signatures to save an allocation.** For example, `scope` parameters on `HandlerMethodArgumentResolver` would break every implementer. Avoid building the object instead: lazy materialisation on a per-endpoint fast path (R-23). | web-http, `73d9ecbf` notes. The fast-path spike (`docs/spring/phase1/fast-path.md` §8.3): of main's 17 allocations on `/greeting`, a `scope` stack allocation could take only 1 (a scratch array in `writeWithMessageConverters`). Every other object reaches an extension point or user code. | Rulebook review |
| R-15 | **Parse from the request buffer; copy only on error.** A value the engine parses and drops (a date, a number, a property name it only looks up) is read as a `scope const(char)[]` slice of the body, through `scope` overloads (`LocalDate.parse(scope const(char)[])`, Jackson's `getStringCharacters`). Only an exception's message copies the text. Property names are interned in a bounded table, so a name already seen costs nothing. | `c812b1b7` (interned names, scratch-buffer unescaping), `1ee6ac1d` (java.time slice overloads) and the Jackson commit after it (dates parse in place). PetFieldsDto 272 → 256 B and VisitFieldsDto 144 → 128 B per read (GC.stats, DMD, local). | DIP1000 `scope` on the parsers; `time_test` overwrites the buffer after parsing |
| R-16 | **Never create an object just to inspect its type.** Decide by the descriptor (`isListLike`, `isMapLike`), then create once. | `c812b1b7`: `readStructured` called `create()` to see whether a type was a collection, then `readBean` created the bean again, so every DTO read built two. The same holds for deciding a scalar: `readScalar` ran two string switches over every JDK scalar name before reaching `string`; testing for String first, and keeping a creator's arguments on the stack (`invoke` takes them `scope`), took petclinic's OwnerFieldsDto read from 18.8k to 16.1k instructions and 2 allocations fewer (callgrind, error-validation request, LDC release, local). | `write_allocations_test`'s "bean read = bean + strings" |
| R-23 | **Specialise provable endpoints and materialise Spring's objects only when something asks.** Flint AOT emits a `fast_<id>` function for a simple mapping. At startup the dispatcher proves, against the strategies it really runs, that generic dispatch would do the same, and fills its content-negotiation table with the generic code itself. The container offers each request before it builds servlet objects. Anything outside the proof is declined before the handler runs, and an exception after it materialises the servlet objects for the resolvers. | `docs/spring/phase1/fast-path.md` (spike, branch `spring/fast-path-proto`): `/greeting` 16 → **1** GC allocation (4,624 → **16** bytes) at `eaa27d1d`, the user's `String.format` result only, the same on DMD and LDC. 18/18 exact on and off. **Certified** (`fast-path-ab`, 4 trials per arm): on ÷ off throughput 1.394, p99 0.507, CPU per request 0.659, RSS 0.999; on ÷ Go throughput 1.293, p99 0.729, CPU 0.843, p50 1.016, RSS 1.45. **Pay-for-use:** only an application with a qualifying endpoint links any of it, through two beans Flint AOT generates. Stripped gs-rest-service: on is +22,664 B; an app with nothing qualifying keeps about 1.1 KB of interface residue, +744 B net against main at `e77bc654`. One pass each for `Accept` and `@RequestParam` (throughput plan change 5): 41,714 → 40,918 worker instructions per `/greeting` (callgrind, LDC release, one reactor, 5,000 requests after 2,000 warm-up). | `fast_path_tests.d` (including "no table without the generated configurer"); the black-box suite on and off; the allocation gate |
| R-24 | **The fast path's fallback rule.** An endpoint takes the generic path unless all three layers hold, and each failure means "generic", never an error. (1) Shape, at build time: one literal `GET` path, no conditions, `@ResponseBody`, no `@ResponseStatus`, no `@CrossOrigin`, a described plain return type (not `ResponseEntity`, `HttpEntity`, `HttpHeaders`, `ProblemDetail`, `Object` or `Any`), `@RequestParam string` arguments only. (2) Proof, at startup: no filters, interceptors, CORS source, custom resolvers or handlers, or `@CrossOrigin`; no return-value handler ahead of `@ResponseBody` supports the return type; the direct-path lookup finds exactly this handler; the resolver composite picks the `@RequestParam` resolver; each `Accept` key selects a parameterless type the Jackson converter writes. (3) Request, at run time: `GET`/`HEAD`, raw path byte-equal to the literal, one `Accept` field among the table's keys, the head within the stack bounds, no missing required parameter. **Decline only before the handler runs**, since running it twice is visible. Every new per-request Spring hook declares how the proof sees it. | fast-path.md §3 and §10 | `fast_path_tests.d` (declines leave the handler unrun) |
| R-25 | **Every module that runs per request designs a build-time direct path, with Spring's objects materialised only when something asks for them** (owner decision `7206d97d`: the AOT fast path is Flint's default execution model, the generic Spring path its fallback). Plan the direct path at the start, not as a later optimisation: what Flint AOT can prove at build time, what the startup proof checks against the configured strategies, and where a request declines before user code runs. Keep responses byte-identical (the black-box suites run on the default), and keep it pay-for-use (generated beans, not always-linked code). | gs-rest-service: certified `fast-path-ab` (R-23). | The black-box suites on the default; the allocation gate; size ratios |
| R-14 | **Don't link a second subsystem by default.** A prototype or alternative engine is opt-in through a version or profile flag until it wins a measurement. | `a84a8c14`: the Flint collector cost about 91 KB at Oz in every program that used `flint:arena`. | Size ratios |
| R-17 | **An engine buffer belongs to the pooled resource, not to each checkout of it.** A statement, parse or write buffer that a connection, stream or request needs is taken from its pool's free list and given back on close. It's never allocated per `getConnection()`, and it's left uninitialized, so only the pages actually used become resident. Rank allocation sites by bytes as well as by count: one buffer can be 92% of the bytes in 0.1% of the allocations. | `15604e2a`: engine-jdbc gave every connection checkout a zero-filled 1 MiB GC buffer. petclinic-rest's GET /owners/1 went from 1,144,256 to 91,584 bytes per request, and list owners from 1.32 MB to 269 KB (the allocation gate, local). The gate's `topAllocationSitesByBytes` found it. | Allocation gate (bytes budgets); engine-jdbc's buffer-reuse test |
| R-18 | **The GC heap follows the live set times druntime's growth policy, not the allocation rate.** druntime adds pools of 1, 4, then 7 MB, and grows instead of collecting while used pages are under twice those that survived the last collection. Quadrupling garbage per request changed only the collection count. Allocation cuts therefore lower collections and pauses; RSS also needs a small live set and smaller pools (`rt_options` `minPoolSize`/`incPoolSize`), which cost pauses at a high allocation rate. | Local, gc.md §1.4: a fixed live set of 200 KB → 5 MB heap, 1 MB → 12 MB, 3 MB → 22 MB, whatever the garbage rate. gs-rest-service's live set is 1.87 MB in a 12 MB heap. With `minPoolSize:1 incPoolSize:0` the heap is 6 MB and peak RSS 16.8–17.0 MB rather than 21.6–22.0, at 2–4× the total pause today. | gc.md measurements; rt_options changes go through proof-harness |
| R-19 | **Size worker pools from the Spring default they stand for, not from connection slots.** Request handlers follow Tomcat's `server.tomcat.threads.max` (200 across all reactor threads, `server.flint.max-handlers`), not one preallocated fiber per connection. Requests past the limit queue on their connections, as Tomcat's executor queues them, and are never refused for want of a handler. Each fiber is a stack plus a guard mapping that the collector also scans. | web-http, the max-handlers commit: on gs-rest-service at 2 reactor threads, idle RSS went from 11.7 to 10.2 MB, peak RSS from 19.3 to 18.5 MB (4 clients × 2,000 requests) and mappings from 4,530 to 580. Local, 3 runs each, same tree with and without the change. ADR-030 §4, "Handler limit". | engine-web `handler_limit` tests: 280 requests past a one-handler limit all answer 200; normal load unchanged |
| R-20 | **Size preallocation by use, not by limits, and keep it off the GC heap.** An engine table reserved for its limits (connection slots, operations, per-fiber cells) is allocated a chunk at a time as slots are first used, in malloc'd memory. Slots are reused lowest first, so a server's working set stays in its first chunks. A chunk that can hold GC pointers is registered as a root before its first pointer is stored and unregistered only at release; a pointer-free table is never scanned. Before cutting, rank the surviving heap by site: `liveAllocationSites` (flint.arena) names what each collection marks. | `2b2c76bf`: engine-reactor's connection, operation, I/O work and FiberLocal tables. On gs-rest-service in-process (2 workers, LDC, local, 2 runs each), the GC live set went from 2,320 to 440 KB and an idle full collection from 149–158 to 104–108 µs (minimum of 200). Release server, 64 connections, 3 runs each: idle RSS 10.2 → 8.3 MB, peak 21.0–21.5 → 19.1–19.6 MB. The heap stays 12 MB under load, which is R-18's policy, so the pool settings come next. Jackson's `JsonFactory` held its 1,024-slot property-name table inline: 8,520 bytes, one 12 KB scanned block in every mapper, and the last Jackson block in gs-rest-service's live set. The table is now allocated by the first read, so a mapper that only writes keeps a 336-byte factory, and a reading app pays exactly 8 KB (two pages, a struct with no array header). engine-sql's C core held its 64-pool table inline: 470 KB of `.bss` inside the writable segment druntime scans on every collection. Pool slots are now allocated when a pool first opens there: petclinic-rest's scanned segment 1,796,408 → 1,342,424 B (−25%), `.bss` 486,028 → 19,084 B, RSS after an 8-connection load 22.0–22.4 → 21.3–22.0 MB. A recycled-buffer pool must be as large as the requests in flight, since a buffer stays borrowed while a send can suspend: the fast path's 16-slot body pool missed on a third of gs-rest-service's requests at 64 connections (one 816-byte buffer per miss); at 256 slots, in-process under oha -c 64 (unittest build), GC bytes per `/greeting` went 418 to 32 (the two user-visible strings) and collections 65 to 5 per 200,000 requests; the local release server's GC heap 12 to 2 MB (CPU per request unchanged locally). | engine-reactor `fiber_local` test: objects held only by lazily allocated chunks survive forced collections, and the test fails with the chunk's `addRange` removed; `operations` chunking and slot reuse; jackson-core `json` unittest: a write leaves the name table unallocated, and two reads return the same interned name |
| R-21 | **A concurrent collector does not pay with a small live set and heavy mutation.** Marking concurrently saves only the marking of the live set, which R-20 made small. The final stop must still rescan everything written meanwhile, which at high request rates is most of the heap, and soft-dirty tracking costs one write fault per page per collection. Shrink the live set and the stop's fixed costs first. Reach for concurrency only for a large, slowly mutating heap. | `259a864d` (D3, `gc:flint-concurrent`), measured locally on gs-rest-service at 64 connections, 3 runs per arm: 137–143k rps against 179k for druntime's collector, p99 2.65–2.75 against 0.91–1.06 ms, pause per collection 1.19–1.30 ms against 0.36–0.38 ms. D1 and D3 are parked, opt-in (coordinator, 2026-11-23); gc.md §3a. | arena `gc_stress` runs under concurrent marking, and its mover test fails without the dirty-page rescan |
| R-22 | **Never build a string to compare type names, and cache by-type bean lookups once the configuration is frozen.** A by-type lookup walks every singleton's class hierarchy; canonicalising each `TypeInfo` name one character at a time made each walk allocate per bean. Canonicalise only when there is something to remove (return the name itself otherwise), and keep Spring's `allBeanNamesByType` as an immutable snapshot published whole, cleared by any registration. | petclinic-rest's allocation gate (local, private PostgreSQL): about 995 allocations per by-type lookup removed; "unknown pet" 1,158 → 163, "update owner" 2,956 → 967, "add pet to owner" 3,331 → 1,342, "list owners" 4,334 → 3,350 (framework-core wave 1). data-access's transaction-manager cache removes the per-call lookup itself. | Allocation gate; `by_type_cache_test` (frozen, invalidation, 8 threads) |
| R-26 | **Size the collector's parallel marking from the CPUs the process may use, not the machine's.** druntime starts `parallel` scan threads up to the host's hardware threads minus one and ignores CPU affinity, so a server pinned to two CPUs of a 32-thread host marks with 31 threads competing for two, and the marking thread waits on them. A Boot application defaults to one mark thread per allowed CPU minus the collecting thread's own (`flint.arena.mark_threads`, a C constructor that puts `gcopt=parallel:<CPUs - 1>` first in `rt_options` before druntime reads it). The program's own `rt_options` and `--DRT-gcopt` still override it. Only this setting changed: pools, a startup collection and musl stay separate experiments. | `gc-pools-ab` (certified, `ce87168d`, FLINT_CORES=2, druntime's collector): `parallel:1` against the default, throughput 1.005, p99 0.809, CPU per request 0.896, max pause 13.5 → 4.0 ms. Locally (LDC and DMD): pinned to CPUs 0,1 the process starts 1 scan thread instead of 31; pinned to one CPU, none instead of 31; unpinned, 31 as before. | arena `mark_threads` test (`rt_options` order, `config.parallel` applied before `main`, the affinity count on Linux); the three apps' acceptance |
| R-27 | **Wake across threads through a reserved per-slot record and a doorbell, never a mailbox post.** A resource released on one worker (a lease, a reply, a completed job) must reach its waiter even while the waiter's owner has every fiber slot busy and its mailbox full, which is when the mailbox stops being dequeued. The waiter arms a `RemoteWaker` before publishing itself and yields; the releaser signals it. One registration ends one yield; a per-owner, monotonic wait key rejects late signals. Owner-local signals stay syscall-free; foreign signals ring the owner's eventfd once per drain. Before this, waiters re-checked every millisecond: one handoff per ms. | Throughput plan change 3. data-access's diagnostic on petclinic-rest (per-pass lease re-checks instead of the 1 ms yield): c=16 from 1,402 to 17,411 req/s, the cap this removes once the lease queue uses the wake (change 4). | engine-reactor `remote_wake` tests: a foreign signal resumes on the next pass on io_uring and epoll; signal before parking; stale and finished-task handles; a full mailbox with no free fiber slot; duplicates ring at most once per drain; a deadline racing a signal resumes once; two owners; stop while signalling; the cross-owner test fails with the doorbell write removed |
| R-40 | **Hand a released pooled resource to its FIFO waiter at once; never let it idle until the waiter's poll.** A strict FIFO pool reserves each freed lease for the queue head, so a head that only re-checks on a timer strands the lease (and every lease freed behind it) for the rest of that tick. Arm the waiter's remote wake (R-27) before its acquisition attempt, register its ticket after the attempt fails, re-check the queue epoch before parking, and have every release, withdrawal and asynchronous retirement signal the lowest registered ticket. The timer becomes a safety net. | engine-sql lease handoff (throughput plan change 4). petclinic-rest GET /owners/1, local release build, server CPUs 0-1, PostgreSQL 4-5, pool of 10: c=16 1,501 → 11,309 req/s (p50 10.5 → 1.4 ms), c=64 1,489 → 16,289 req/s (p50 43 → 3.7 ms); a released lease idles 49-52 µs per handoff instead of 708-746 µs (owner-1 counters, sql.handoff); server CPU stays about 0.1 ms per request; 5,000 req/s fixed rate at c=64 holds (p50 0.49 ms). | engine-sql offload tests: 256 fibers on 10 leases over 2 workers; a withdrawn head's successor gets the next release within 30 ms; a connection killed mid-lease hands its slot to the waiter within 50 ms |
| R-28 | **A table sized for its limit is scanned only over its live entries.** A lookup that walks every slot of a table reserved for its maximum costs that maximum on every call, however few slots are used. Keep live entries dense (an index list plus a free stack) and scan those, or index directly. | engine-reactor native watches: the SQL wait registers and removes a watch on each libpq wait, and each call scanned all 1,024 entries. petclinic-rest GET /petclinic/api/owners/1, serial, one reactor, callgrind on LDC release at `f2a1cd80`: 760.6k to 613.6k instructions per request (-19.3%); `NativeWatches.update` 146k to 0.6k. | engine-reactor `native_watch` tests: a full table refuses a new descriptor, updates a live one without a free entry, reuses a removed entry, and reports only watched descriptors, on epoll and io_uring |
| R-29 | **A request condition that matches whole returns the mapping's own condition.** Spring builds a new matching condition for every candidate mapping on every request; most match whole, so the result equals the registered one. Return the registered slice (read only) when every expression matches, and build a narrowed one only for a partial match. Conditions are checked in Spring's order before the path, so the cost multiplies by the candidate mappings. | `RequestMappingInfo.matchProduces` (the path patterns already did this): petclinic-rest GET /petclinic/api/owners/1, serial, one reactor, callgrind on LDC release: 262 to 239 GC allocations per request and 613.6k to 584.6k instructions (-4.7%). petclinic-rest 109/109 and gs-rest-service 18/18 (fast path on and off), wire exact. The produces lookup also resolves the `Accept` values directly, as Spring's default manager would, instead of building a manager and request wrapper per lookup; with `HttpHeaders.EMPTY()` allocating nothing and the servlet request's `getHeaders` sizing its result once, gs-rest-service `GET /nope` (`Accept: */*`) went from 202.1k to 196.0k instructions and 68 to 59 GC allocations per request; gs-rest 18/18 (fast path on and off) and petclinic 109/109. | framework-test `request_mapping_info_tests`: a whole match is the mapping itself, a partial match a new info that then matches whole, `Accept: */*` the mapping itself |
| R-30 | **Prepare per-type metadata once, and build per-violation objects only when a violation exists.** Hibernate reads each bean's constraints into `BeanMetaData` once. A port that reads annotations, compiles `@Pattern`, creates the `ConstraintDescriptor` and builds every property path on each `validate` pays for the configuration on every request. Prepare a type on its first validation (copy-on-write table keyed by its descriptor, so lookups take no lock); drop annotations that are not constraints; make a property path only for a violation, a cascade or an exception message. | The validator change (this rule's commit): petclinic-rest `POST /petclinic/api/owners` with one invalid field (the `error-validation-p99` request; callgrind, LDC release, one reactor, 1,000 requests after 300, local). Hibernate's `validate` went from 285k to 46k instructions and from 438 to 15 allocation calls per request. The whole request went from 717k to 462k instructions and from 755 to 300 allocation calls. The 400 body is byte-identical and petclinic-rest's acceptance passes 109/109 (wire exact). Executable validation (`@Validated` controllers) kept building the method and parameter path and re-preparing each parameter's constraints on every call, valid or not; prepared once per element (keyed by bean type, method, parameter types and slot: a first cut keyed by the annotation array's address missed on every call, because proxies' descriptors are rebuilt, and turned petclinic's allocation gate red until `ValidatorFactoryImpl.preparedFor` moved to the element's name and compare-and-swap slots) and with lazy executable paths, petclinic's `GET /owners/1` went from 556k to 538k instructions and from 198 to 182 GC allocations per request (`validateParameters` 8.6k to 1.2k, `validateReturnValue` 6.8k to 0.3k). A variant that delayed resumed tasks' writes to the end of the resume pass, to let database continuations run first, did no better on petclinic and was dropped. | engine-reactor `send_on_return` (two tasks of one pass: the second finds the first's response already at the peer, on epoll and io_uring; it fails with the end-of-pass start), the io_uring cases of `write_now` (whole, larger than the socket buffers, EAGAIN first, closed peer), and engine-reactor, engine-web, engine-servlet and webmvc suites with natives (io_uring) on DMD and LDC |

It appends the ranges to keep (D variables, array-literal storage, C data, `.bss`) after the ELF image, with a checksummed trailer, and `flint.arena` validates them and applies them at startup. **Trap:** `spring_bench`'s `pinned_environment` drops `FLINT_*` variables, so an A/B toggles the map with binary variants, not `FLINT_GC_STATIC_TRIM`. | Posidon at O3, petclinic validation 400, same binary with no map, the provable list and the full list, 2 rounds: pause per collection 264.0/260.5 → 238.5/236.9 µs (−9.5%), mark 244/240 → 218/217 µs. The provable list alone: 262.3/267.1 µs. CPU per request and RSS unchanged. The map step costs 2–5 ms per link. | Three layers: FLINT-E0536's typed gate (tools/check_class_monitor.py: rejects, accepts, suppressions, the whole repository); the audit watcher in debug and test builds (static_audit.d's negative test names the class); startup validation with fallback (tests/test_flint_static_map.py: release applies, dev audits, `FLINT_GC_STATIC_TRIM=0` and `=provable`, a damaged map is mismatched, a stripped one absent, an object held only by a `__gshared` survives). arena suites on DMD and LDC; flint CLI tests; acceptance 109/109 and 18/18 with maps applied; both apps' allocation gates. |
| R-108 | **Make the façade a handle when its storage is already guarded.** Every `ServletEngineRequest` method already enters the exchange's generation guard (R-97), so nothing the request says or changes needs to live in the object user code holds. The request line, the fields and body, redirects, attributes and parameters moved into the pooled `RequestState`; the façade keeps the host, the state and its generation and is asserted at most 64 bytes (256 before, a 256-byte block). A retained request still throws `IllegalStateException`, because every method still checks the generation before touching the state. | venus, 46abba61 against 46abba61 plus this change, ThinLTO codegen at O3, spring_instr: petclinic validation 400 3,696 to 3,504 bytes per request (40 allocations both), 102,825 to 102,930 instructions (+0.1%, one more load per field read). gs-rest-service 18/18 and petclinic-rest 109/109 wire exact; engine-servlet (native), webmvc, framework-test and boot-web-server on DMD and LDC; both apps' request-allocation gates on LDC and DMD; structural gate. | servlet_engine_tests' adversarial and many-slots tests (retained requests from the next exchange's fiber and from another thread; redirects, attributes and parameters past the inline slots) unchanged and passing |
| R-109 | **Decode and box SpEL literals when the expression is parsed.** A standalone literal keeps the scalar `Any`. A numeric expression keeps the operators and typed operands in evaluation order, so the interpreter uses a bounded stack of numbers and boxes only the result. Power uses the scalar `pow` entry point and applies Java narrowing without boxing either operand. Scalar comparisons and unary signs use the same evaluator. Direct signed numeric literals retain their parse-time values, and a computed sign updates the top of the evaluation stack. Boolean operators jump over an unneeded right operand on that stack, so the right operand has no evaluation cost or side effects. Ternary and Elvis branch on the same stack and preserve the chosen scalar type. Cache text operands at parse time and initialize only a 16-entry stack for expressions with fewer than 32 instructions. | The expression probe on venus went from 28,512 linked bytes and 1,007 template instances at d34c47df to 42,152 bytes and 1,228 instances with numeric operators. Scalar power moved the same probe from 59,896 to 63,992 linked bytes and from 1,312 to 1,314 instances, with the probe workload and toolchain unchanged. Scalar comparisons then measured 68,088 linked bytes and 1,351 instances. Short-circuit logic measured 76,312 bytes and 1,364 instances. DMD and LDC check `nothrow @nogc` evaluation for quoted strings, mixed-width arithmetic and scalar power. Computed signs measured 72,010 incremental loadable bytes and 1,365 template instances on venus under the new loadable-section metric at 520f8f11. Optimized DMD and LDC runs allocated zero GC bytes over 40,000 warmed public evaluations of a negated float expression on each compiler. The combined context and conditional probe measured 75,314 incremental loadable bytes and 1,417 template instances. A fixture exercises 200 levels with two pending operands per level and checks rejection at level 201. | `framework/expression/tests/literal_tests.d`, `framework/expression/tests/operator_tests.d` and the module's size and template gate |

The stripped O3 feature and control were 356,424 B and 340,320 B in the diagnostic build. | UTF-16 ordering and unequal-length tests in `tags_test.d` and the Micrometer module's linked-size gate. |

Lookup and dirty checking allocate zero GC bytes. The unchanged probe (45d98a0b against e63051aa) stayed at 5,508 loadable bytes at O3 and 5,076 at Oz, with DMD templates increasing from 855 to 876. Flush stages the exact bound values in the alternate bank and retains GC-owned payloads in reserved scratch until JDBC consumes them. A one-row update and successful version write publish the bank, and a failed stage leaves the active baseline available. The unchanged engine probe (`a136c0341` against `5655d9c89`) measured 5,596 loadable bytes at O3, 5,164 at Oz, and 1,012 DMD templates in both builds. Check the pending UPDATE stage after parameter binding and before execution. Both paths can retry. The unchanged engine probe (`84334da7f` against `4df04291a`) measured 5,596 loadable bytes at O3, 5,164 at Oz, and 1,012 DMD templates in both builds. The loaded version comes from the original slot. The pinned PostgreSQL 18.7 fixture checks all three preflight callbacks, preserves the replacement baseline, and measures zero GC bytes on rejection on both compilers. Matched engine probes (`c07c0b04f` against `3cb4b0189`) kept O3 at 5,596 loadable bytes and Oz at 5,164 while DMD templates fell from 1,012 to 1,009. Reject an overlapping UPDATE on the same attachment before mapped reads or JDBC. The PostgreSQL fixture attempts a nested staged or nonrefresh call from the outer transaction check. Both compilers show zero GC bytes on rejection, one transaction check, no nested mapped name read, and a successful outer and subsequent update. DMD instances rose from 916 to 920 within the existing 929 cap. Both optimized compilers checked 320,000 successful guards with zero GC bytes. | Existing module budgets and pinned Java comparisons covering typed null, empty inputs, nonempty inputs with null elements, and exact failure messages. |

Each optimized compiler ran 160,000 array and erased-list checks with zero GC bytes. Generic typed-list checks added no allocation beyond the inherited iterator. | Existing module budgets and pinned Java parity for absent and empty containers and the caller's null-element diagnostic. |

The one-byte-short cases failed with zero arena consumption. | The CLI Thymeleaf lowering tests and the engine-template render and capacity tests. |
| R-181 | **Count a template budget by call site and by operand shape, and keep error paths shape-neutral.** jackson-core's budget counts every instantiation, not distinct ones. Every `new` site is a `_d_newclassT` instance and every runtime `~` site a `_d_arraycatnTX` instance. A new concatenation shape also brings its helpers (`__arrayAlloc`, `Unqual`, `hasElaborateCopyConstructor`), three more. The operand's qualifier (`immutable string` vs `string`), whether it is an lvalue or an rvalue (the template takes `auto ref`), and a compile-time `~` of `enum` strings each make a new shape. Slice 1 of the read features (comments, RS, trailing commas, missing values) first cost 611 against a cap of 600. It came back to the base count by funnelling constructions through one helper each (end-of-input, constraints) and writing new messages in the module's existing literal/string/literal/string shape. A `/` `#` control-byte table also replaced `skipWhitespace`'s four compares. | dmd -vtemplates=list-instances on jackson-core, base vs candidate: 561 vs 561 (an intermediate 611, then 571, then 564). spring_instr on venus at O3, the validation 400 against fp/main: 103,143 to 103,171 instructions, 40 allocations and 3,504 B unchanged. | jackson-core DoD budgets step (template_instances); CommentParsingTest, TrailingCommasTest, TrailingCommas616Test, NonStandardAllowRSTest |
| R-182 | **A cold path must not add a caller to a hot callee that the compiler inlines.** LDC inlined `JsonFactory.internName` into the strict `readName` while it had one caller. Slice 2's lenient names called it as well, and with two callers LDC stopped inlining it: the validation 400's JSON parse cost a call and lost its inlined compare (+114 instructions in Jackson's layer). The cold path now stores an owned copy, so the hot path keeps its single inlined call. Routing the hot `.idup` sites through a shared helper had the same effect, so those stay inline. A related trap: `dchar.init` is U+FFFF, not 0, so an accumulator declared `dchar unit;` decodes `\uD800` as 0xFFFFD800. | spring_instr on venus at O3, the validation 400 against read features slice 1: 103,134 to 103,196 instructions (it was +114 to +156 before the fix), 40 allocations and 3,504 B unchanged. jackson-core's templates went from 561 to 560 by the budget step's count. | jackson-core DoD (budgets); NonStandardAposQuotedNamesTest's lone-surrogate case |
| R-183 | **Keep a cold feature's per-token state behind one sentinel field.** A strict number now pays one store (`_oddText = null`). The non-standard forms' display text, parse digits and NaN flag are read only when that field is set. A class holding them cost five template instances (its type info and two `new` sites). `~=` appends cost five more, so the digits are copied into the scratch buffer through one writer. The forms are leading `+`, leading zeros, leading and trailing decimal points, and NaN and the Infinities. | jackson-core templates 560 to 560 (dmd -vtemplates). spring_instr on venus at O3, the validation 400 against slice 2: 103,183 to 103,107 instructions, 40 allocations and 3,504 B unchanged. | jackson-core DoD (budgets); NonStandardNumberParsingTest, NonStandardLeadingPlusSign784Test |
| R-184 | **Build an error's variable text in the scratch buffer, not with `~`.** Jackson's "Unrecognized token 'xyz...'" adds "..." only when the token is cut at 256 UTF-16 units, and its expected-value list depends on ALLOW_NON_NUMERIC_NUMBERS. A ternary operand in the concatenation (`~ (cut ? "..." : "") ~`) added one `_d_arraycatnTX` instance, and a named `string` for it added seven (a new shape and its helpers). Writing the whole message into the scratch buffer through `putScratch` and copying it once with `owned` needs no concatenation, so the count fell by one. The root-number check stays one table read on the hot path (`numberEnd[c]`, then the context only for ',', ']', '}' or ':'); Jackson's `_verifyRootSpace`, `_checkMatchEnd` and identifier rules run only after it fails. | jackson-core templates 560 to 559 (dmd -vtemplates). spring_instr on venus at O3, the validation 400 against read features slice 3 (c3f2b4368): 103,099 to 103,114 instructions, 40 allocations and 3,504 B unchanged. jackson-core's functions cost the same in callgrind; the difference is libc memcmp under `ServletEngineRequest.setAttribute` (string-literal alignment). | jackson-core DoD (budgets); root_values_test |
| R-197 | **Build a cold error message in one scratch writer, not with `~` at each site.** The parser's new error texts (Jackson's unexpected-character, constraint, invalid-UTF-8 and unrecognized-token messages) each concatenated at their own site, and with LTO every site's array-concatenation call and argument setup links into every application through `UTF8StreamJsonParser`. One out-of-line `joined(scope const(char)[][] pieces...)` writes the pieces into the scratch buffer and copies once, so a site is a stack array of slices and a call; a `scope` typesafe variadic allocates nothing and instantiates no template. The same pass dropped a 512-byte hex-digit table for two computed digits, merged two Latin-1 name tables into one bit-flag table, and gave the cold paths one out-of-line UTF-8 decoder instead of three inlined copies. | The webmvc framework-size probe (O3, the merge train's module probe) against main 3269ba86: +16,736 loadable bytes before, +14,504 after, under the 1% allowance for a module already over its cap; the parser's symbols shrank by 2,333 bytes. jackson-core templates 560 to 510 (dmd -vtemplates). | tools/module_probes.py (merge train); jackson-core DoD (budgets) |
| R-192 | **Exchange published GC references in one atomic operation.** Use a strong compare-and-exchange to return the reference observed at the comparison. A reference comparison uses object identity. | Pinned LDC O3 IR shows acquire/release for the existing slot calls and sequentially consistent instructions for the new operations. The O3 and Oz probes admit zero Phobos archive members. The conc package probe moves from 1,270 to 1,325 DMD templates against the 1,500 cap. The Java volatile leaf probe adds 1,085 linked bytes at O3 and Oz and uses 47 DMD templates. The publication probe remains at 973 linked bytes and uses 49 templates. | Concurrent old-value and interface identity tests, GC-liveness checks, the canonical Linux sanitizer profile, and the linkage probes. |
| R-199 | **Copy the leading single-byte run directly between backing arrays before entering a buffer-by-buffer coder loop.** In `java.nio.charset` decoders and encoders (`US-ASCII`, `ISO-8859-1`, `UTF-8`), calling `ByteBuffer.get()` / `CharBuffer.put()` per unit pays virtual dispatch and bounds/cursor updates on every byte. When both buffers have accessible arrays, scan and widen/narrow the leading ASCII (or full Latin-1) run directly across the slices, advance both positions once, and hand off to the ported buffer loop for the stopping unit. | Venus, LDC 1.43.0 `-O3 -release`, deterministic callgrind `I refs` over 500 × 4,096-byte ASCII buffers (2,048,000 units), buffer loop (`b4a2e62f1`) vs leading array run (`9efa173d5`): UTF-8 decode 285.9M → 19.7M (−93.1%, 14.5×), UTF-8 encode 292.0M → 19.8M (−93.2%, 14.8×), US-ASCII decode 337.1M → 19.8M (−94.1%, 17.1×), US-ASCII encode 290.0M → 19.7M (−93.2%, 14.7×), ISO-8859-1 decode 304.3M → 2.53M (−99.2%, 120×, auto-vectorized), ISO-8859-1 encode 318.6M → 19.8M (−93.8%, 16.1×). All four `java-nio-charset` leaf budgets pass under v4.2 (`charsetdecoder` 48,132 O3 / 45,668 Oz B / 518 templates; `charsetencoder` 60,584 O3 / 57,972 Oz B / 545 templates). | `framework/java-nio-charset/tests/single_byte_run_test.d` (exact parity against buffer loop on DMD and LDC), `standard_coders_test.d` (1,811-line Temurin 21.0.13+9 oracle), and `framework/java-nio-charset/leaf-budgets.json`. |
| R-200 | **Park a Java Future waiter on the task's own state word and publish the result before waking it.** `FutureTask` keeps Java's NEW/RUNNING/COMPLETING/NORMAL/EXCEPTIONAL/CANCELLED state in one shared 32-bit word. `run` stores the value or failure, then the state with release ordering, then wakes every waiter; `get`/`get(timeout)` re-read the state and call `flint.conc.park` on that word, so an owner fiber yields to its reactor instead of spinning, and a thread parks in the kernel. The leaf also keeps `RunnableFuture` in the Future leaf and rejects a null task in the constructor. | The provider-equivalent probe on this box measured 11,884 O3 / 11,184 Oz incremental loadable bytes and 490 joint DMD template instances; the first caps are 13,073 bytes and 539 templates. The existing package caps 7,480 bytes and 494 templates are unchanged because the package probe does not link the leaf. | `framework/java-concurrent-exec/tests/future_task_test.d` (run, failure, cancel, timeout, and a cross-thread get), the closure probe `closure/futuretask.d`, and `leaf-budgets.json`. |
| R-201 | **Declare scheduled futures as interfaces over the existing Delayed and Future leaves.** A scheduled future is Java's `ScheduledFuture`/`RunnableScheduledFuture` contract only: it adds no state and no engine. Both interfaces extend the `flint.java.util.concurrent.delayed` and `flint.java.util.concurrent.future` leaves, so an app that never schedules a task links neither the scheduling engine nor the delay queue. The probe implements the inherited APIs in its control without the new interfaces, so the cap charges only this leaf's declarations. | The provider-equivalent probe measured 3,344 O3 / 3,320 Oz incremental loadable bytes and 345 joint DMD template instances; the first caps are 3,679 bytes and 380 templates. The existing package caps 7,480 bytes and 494 templates are unchanged because the package probe does not link the leaf. | `framework/java-concurrent-exec/tests/scheduled_future_test.d`, the closure probe `closure/scheduledfuture.d`, and `leaf-budgets.json`. |
| R-202 | **Expose a generic Java method on an interface as a template that builds the already-budgeted task object, not as an erased primitive.** Java's `ExecutorService.submit` is abstract on the interface and implemented by `AbstractExecutorService` over `execute`; D cannot make an interface's generic method virtual. Each `submit` form is a body-bearing template method on the interface that constructs the existing `FutureTask!T` and passes it to `execute`, so the value type flows through without an erasure cast, and an application that never calls `submit` instantiates nothing: the service leaf's own link closure keeps no `FutureTask` symbols. The value-less form is a template with `T = Object` so its `Future<?>` wildcard erasure also stays uninstantiated until used. | The `java-concurrent-service` link-closure probe (which still exercises only execute/shutdown) measured 47,868 O3 / 42,548 Oz incremental loadable bytes and 811 joint DMD templates, against the unchanged caps of 52,664 bytes and 868 templates; before this slice the same probe measured 47,876 / 42,556 and 789, so the added declarations cost no linked bytes and stay inside the cap headroom. The package probe measures 47,748 bytes and 1,355 owned templates (from 47,756 / 1,321 before the slice) against the unchanged 52,532-byte and 1,454-template caps, and its bench fixture never calls `submit`. The submit path reuses the already-budgeted `futuretask` leaf (13,073 bytes / 539 templates). | The `java-concurrent-service` definition-of-done `link-closure` and `budgets` steps; `leaf-budgets.json`; `framework/java-concurrent-service/tests/executor_service_test.d`. |
| R-203 | **Implement a collection-of-tasks Java method as an interface template that submits the already-budgeted task object.** Java's `ExecutorService.invokeAll` is abstract on the interface and implemented by `AbstractExecutorService`; D cannot make an interface's generic method virtual. Both `invokeAll` forms are body-bearing template methods that wrap each `Callable!T` in the existing `FutureTask!T` and pass it to `execute`, so an application that never calls `invokeAll` instantiates nothing and the service leaf's link closure stays unchanged. The timed form carries AbstractExecutorService's rule that a timeout cancels the unfinished futures and returns them instead of throwing. | The `java-concurrent-service` link-closure probe (execute/shutdown only) measures no change: the new methods are templates and the leaf already budgets `FutureTask` and `Future`. The package probe likewise keeps its caps; the `submit` path's 47,748-byte / 1,355-template measurement against the unchanged 52,532-byte and 1,454-template caps still holds. | The `java-concurrent-service` definition-of-done `link-closure` and `budgets` steps; `framework/java-concurrent-service/tests/executor_service_test.d`. |
| R-204 | **A one-shot Java scheduler is a managed worker over a sorted deadline queue that parks on a version word, and it charges only its marginal engine cost.** `ScheduledExecutorService.schedule` is a template on the interface that builds the already-budgeted `ScheduledTask` over `FutureTask` and hands it to a package hook; the concrete `OffloadScheduledExecutorService` keeps sorted parallel arrays (no helper class, so LDC does not pull the new module into the existing `executorservice` probe's first object), a `Mutex` plus a 32-bit version word, and a managed background thread that parks outside the lock and wakes on enqueue, cancel and shutdown. `shutdownNow` cancels and returns the pending commands; `shutdown` drains the remaining deadlines before the worker exits; `isTerminated` is the worker's finished flag. | The provider-equivalent control implements the same queue and worker directly on `flint.conc`, `FutureTask` and `ExecutorService` without the leaf. On the actual base (after dropping an unlanded foreign base from this stack) the leaf probe measured 15,272 O3 / 13,740 Oz incremental loadable bytes and 986 joint DMD template instances; first caps are ceil(measured x 1.10) = 16,800 bytes and 1,085 templates. The package probe compiles the new module: 53,988 linked bytes and 1,459 templates, so its first caps are 59,387 bytes and 1,605 templates (the lower of the leaf sum and measured plus 10%). The existing service leaf was re-measured on the same base at 54,124 O3 / 49,164 Oz bytes and 813 templates, so its first byte cap is 59,537; the earlier 52,664 was measured against a base that is not on main. | `framework/java-concurrent-service/tests/scheduled_executor_service_test.d`, the closure probe `closure/scheduledexecutorservice.d`, `leaf-budgets.json`, and the `java-concurrent-service` definition-of-done `link-closure` and `budgets` steps. |

## 2. Binary size

| ID | Rule | Evidence | Enforced by |
|---|---|---|---|
| S-117 | **Keep optional executor services behind their own archives.** Route bounded admission through a scalar permit and claim the command on the managed offload worker. Share erased List storage for the shutdown snapshot. | With pinned DMD and LDC on venus at the erased List rehearsal `f6d53fed0`, the service adds 47,756/42,420 loadable bytes at O3/Oz and 1,321 package-owned templates. Its leaf adds 47,876/42,556 bytes and 789 templates in the gate’s joint invocation. Rejection measures 380/876 bytes and 441 package-owned templates, and 420/916 bytes and 225 joint leaf templates. The first probes seed the exact 34 legacy module records under decision `523bd5bd`. | Provider-equivalent size probes, enforced leaf budgets, and scoped DMD/LDC offload and shutdown tests. |
| S-1 | **Pay for what you use, through reachability.** D keeps every virtual method of a linked class alive through its vtable. Code that only some apps need, such as date parsing or `@JsonFormat` patterns, must not be reachable from an always-linked virtual or class. Put it behind a module that registration links: Jackson's `Module` API, an AOT-generated customizer, or a templated façade. Mark API methods `final` where upstream allows, so they leave the vtable. | Jackson Date went from +16% (+80 KB) to +2.6% on the jackson-databind probe (`5e48603a`, `bc95dae5`, `20493652`); every app paid about 70 KB before and 9–17 KB after. Diagnosis: `--print-gc-sections` and linker maps. The Period fixture on venus adds 13,628 loadable bytes at O3 and 11,388 at Oz, compared with stripped file deltas of 18,928 and 14,832 bytes. The exact loadable-byte cap is 14,991 and the fixture counts 948 DMD template instances. With MonthDay in the same archive, Period's non-template copy for rendered and rejected text keeps MonthDay module metadata out of the Period probe. On the same two-leaf candidate the probe fell from 12,980/10,740 bytes to 12,844/10,612 at O3/Oz. The original ValueRange bounds probe adds 6,652 and 5,148 loadable bytes at O3 and Oz on venus. With the full Java hash result in the probe, it adds 6,740 and 5,212 bytes and counts 883 DMD templates in the isolated library and probe. Both compilers verify zero GC bytes on construction, bound checks and hashing. The separate ValueRange display probe adds 7,212 loadable bytes at O3 and 5,676 at Oz with 883 DMD template instances. It renders to a stack buffer with one owned result allocation. On the dependency set at `bc89af1c`, display measured 7,380/5,844 bytes at O3/Oz. Sharing one non-inlined digit loop reduced those deltas to 7,100/5,628 under the unchanged 7,318-byte cap. ChronoField numeric metadata adds 3,124/1,948 bytes at O3/Oz with the existing ValueRange link dependency. The name-only probe adds 1,128 bytes in both profiles and retains neither the numeric range table nor another Java leaf. The isolated leaf plus either probe counts 886 DMD instances. The new caps are 3,437 bytes and 975 instances. ChronoUnit duration adds 2,316/1,140 O3/Oz bytes and names add 520 in both profiles. The leaf and either probe count 872 DMD instances, with caps of 2,548 bytes and 960 instances. ChronoField unit ids add 224 bytes in both profiles and link neither the duration nor the name table. YearMonth parse and display add 13,008/9,768 O3/Oz bytes, with 442 DMD templates. Arithmetic adds 11,428/8,588 bytes and retains no YearMonth parser or renderer. Parsing valid scoped text allocates no GC bytes. Rendering owns one result. Leaf caps are 14,309 bytes and 487 templates. MonthDay parse/render adds 13,060/10,164 loadable bytes at O3/Oz, calendar operations 10,980/8,140, and either isolated probe counts 440 DMD templates. Year composition adds 12,044/9,204 loadable bytes and 444 templates. Year parse/render adds 9,684/7,156 bytes and 442 templates and leaves both MonthDay and YearMonth out of the link closure. | Per-package linked-bytes ratio (1.05) and ceilings |
| S-1a | **Native subsystems are linked by composition, never by a runtime branch.** A C shim that dispatches on a runtime value (`if (driver == POSTGRES)`) keeps every branch's library: `--gc-sections` cannot drop code a reachable function calls. Put each optional native subsystem behind an enabler that is its only reference (`enableTls()`, `enableHttp2()`, `enablePostgres()`, `enableSqlite()`), and have the composition call it: the app's declared dependency (`flint:postgresql`, `flint:sqlite-jdbc`, Spring's driver jar), whose `META-INF/services` provider Flint AOT links, so an undeclared driver fails at startup. Diagnose with a symbol breakdown by origin and a reachability graph over code and RELR pointers. | SQL drivers (this commit): gs-relational-data-access Oz 8,359,904 → 4,400,528 B stripped (Go 6,695,800: 1.249 → 0.657), no libpq, SSL or EVP symbols; petclinic-rest 12,173,112 → 10,776,280 B (Go 11,380,728: 1.070 → 0.947), no SQLite symbols. Before: certified at `6ab588f8`; after: local Oz build on the change. web-http's TLS and HTTP/2 split took gs-rest-service from 8,237,064 to 4,488,192 B (`99646772`, `98e97ba4`). | Oz against Go in gs-builds; `nm` checks in the change; per-package linked-bytes ceilings of `flint:postgresql` and `flint:sqlite-jdbc` (their probes construct the driver) |
| S-1b | **Check JDK reachability per public leaf and operation.** Include local symbols and live module metadata when auditing the optimized link. Sum file-backed allocated sections to keep page alignment out of the measurement. | Venus, `17d2aacb`, LDC 1.43.0 with canonical O3/Oz ThinLTO and GNU ld 2.42. `Integer.parseInt` retained 11 Java modules and added 6,484/5,028 loadable bytes, while `classOf` added zero. The D array fallback in `valueOf` retained all 13 banned Phobos families and added 473,683/472,291 B. [Probe baseline](../../framework/java/closure/README.md). | JDK link-closure and structural import ratchets |
| S-1c | **Keep the D string-array display behind its own formatter.** Size its output once and instantiate the immutable copy in the calling template so Java's archive cannot supply an unrelated weak array helper. Link the generated Unicode ranges through this formatter. | On venus with LDC 1.43.0 and canonical O3/Oz ThinLTO, the same `valueOf(string[])` probe at `17d2aacb` and this slice goes from 473,683/472,291 to 11,372/11,324 incremental loadable bytes. The 32 core probes report 26 fewer violations across both profiles. | JDK closure probe and string-array display tests with DMD and LDC |

`framework-test` also exhibits S-9. The O3 link map at `e4e8ac8a` shows a `std.json.JSONValue` associative-array helper referenced by `flint.mock.web.o` resolving from Phobos `curl.o`. That member pulls in socket, regex and concurrency code. On venus, `tools/measure_builds.py --framework-sizes --framework framework-test` measured 1,748,022 incremental loadable bytes both before and after the status matcher port. DMD library-plus-probe template instances fell from 9,910 to 9,905. The status range matcher formats its diagnostic only on failure and uses the existing Java integer formatter. The 03:33Z S-9 ratchet applies while this cascade remains, with an absolute cap of 1,552,384 bytes.
Direct directory enumeration keeps filesystem identities in a separate entry record, so ordinary file facts retain their existing layout. The engine-aot probe on venus, from Boot's cursor 2274185b to 9a240f47, kept 4,916 loadable bytes and moved DMD template instances from 762 to 774 against the 1,100 cap. The directory entry tests cover the scan bound, path failures and link identity.

The MockMvc header and content matcher port reuses Jackson for comparison, `HttpHeaders` for date parsing, and Java `Long` for the numeric header assertion. On venus the unchanged `framework-test` probe moved from 1,748,022 to 1,748,142 incremental loadable bytes against `cbf1e27b`. DMD library-plus-probe templates moved from 9,905 to 9,973, within the 12,450 cap. The S-9 ratchet covers the 120-byte growth of this API slice.

MockMvc compiles each JSON path when it creates the matcher and evaluates it against Jackson. The unchanged `framework-test` probe on venus moved from 1,744,390 to 1,742,678 incremental loadable bytes and from 9,255 to 8,322 DMD library-plus-probe templates against `3c6e97ad`. The O3 link map still shows `flint.mock.web.o` pulling Phobos `exception.o`, which retains `json.o` and `curl.o`. The S-9 ratchet applies while the cascade keeps the probe over its 1,552,384-byte absolute cap.

MockMvc reuses `UriComponentsBuilder` for the authority and path. It keeps raw query text and percent escapes, and sets the decoded servlet path info when building the mock. The unchanged venus `framework-test` probe at `0228a408` and the URI port measured 1,742,678 and 1,755,070 loadable bytes, respectively. DMD library-plus-probe templates rose from 8,322 to 9,342 against a cap of 12,450. The candidate O3 map still shows Phobos `exception.o` retaining `curl.o`. The 12,392-byte growth is measured against the S-9 base-relative ratchet, with an absolute cap of 1,552,384 bytes.
ResourceRegion validates its coordinates without opening a stream. Its independent Spring Core module follows S-1. On venus, the pinned DMD and LDC probe (`tools/measure_builds.py --framework-sizes --framework core`) measured 8,708 loadable bytes on both d89572cf and its ResourceRegion candidate, with DMD templates moving from 6,387 to 6,389 against the 8,550 cap.

InputStreamResource reuses Java's stream and Object operations. On the same Core probe, 0016ed2e to a8182248 kept 8,708 loadable bytes and moved DMD templates from 6,389 to 6,407.

BeanDefinitionStoreException retains the resource description as an R5 nullable value. The Beans probe on venus against `ad0405d8f` moved from 257,478 to 258,430 loadable bytes and from 1,632 to 1,688 DMD template instances against the 3,000 cap.

LocalizedResourceHelper keeps locale fallback behind the Core resource support module. The same probe on venus, from its parent 0016ed2e to 05b6c659, kept 8,708 loadable bytes and moved DMD templates from 6,389 to 6,472, within the 8,550 cap. The ResourceRegion, InputStreamResource, CompositeIterator and LocalizedResourceHelper batch on 37c78eea kept the Core probe at 8,884 loadable bytes and moved DMD templates from 6,392 to 6,495.

Multipart boundary generation uses `flint.conc.fillSecureRandom` from the method body. On venus, the Core size probe from `b360ec62` to this port moved from 8,844 to 19,197 loadable bytes and from 5,491 to 5,553 DMD templates. Both stay below their caps of 81,920 bytes and 8,550 templates. The Spring apps already link `conc` through the reactor. Their app budgets are checked by the merge train.

Character-to-number conversion normalizes the UTF-16 code unit to a signed short inside the existing erased numeric converter. It shares target range checks and needs no additional converter registration. The Core probe on venus against `dd109d0e` kept 8,844 loadable bytes and moved from 5,491 to 5,502 DMD template instances against the 8,550 cap.

SimpleRouteMatcher retains the original route string and delegates matching, extraction and ordering to its configured PathMatcher. The Core probe on venus against `b63261658` kept 8,844 loadable bytes and moved from 5,491 to 5,510 DMD template instances against the 8,550 cap.

StreamUtils copies and drains through one heap buffer per transfer, and its wrappers forward bulk reads and writes. The Core probe on venus against the rebased batch parent `837240684` moved from 19,197 to 19,237 loadable bytes and from 5,583 to 5,613 DMD template instances against caps of 81,920 bytes and 8,550 instances.

Servlet response sinks that override whole-array writes need an explicit ranged bulk write when the Java `OutputStream` defaults dispatch ranged writes through `write(int)`. `StreamUtils.copy(InputStream, OutputStream)` calls the ranged overload. In a local venus probe on DMD and LDC, copying 4,096 bytes through a counting sink on `3466eaa20` made one bulk call. With the Java dispatch draft `621bcb3ab`, a whole-array-only override received 4,096 single-byte calls. Adding the ranged override restored one bulk call, with identical output in all cases. This probe counts calls on a servlet-shaped sink. The engine-servlet ranged-write test verifies that sending four selected bytes through a two-byte response buffer emits one body chunk. The combined servlet change also runs the app-train allocation and budget gates.

BindingResultUtils looks up the prefixed model key through the Java map's existing erased view. It formats the attribute only on a type error. The Context probe on venus against `49a07739d` kept 631,100 loadable bytes and moved from 5,010 to 5,029 DMD template instances against the 9,350 cap.

DefaultResourceLoader decodes file URL paths through `StringUtils.uriDecode`. The isolated Core probe on venus against `7bfedc4c7` kept 8,844 loadable bytes and 5,491 DMD template instances. The Context probe on the combined lane batch against `de5f66684` moved from 631,548 to 633,672 loadable bytes and kept 5,037 DMD template instances against the 9,350 cap.

MimeTypeUtils calls the entropy leaf directly. On venus, paired against leaf-only `7e26ef1bd`, the unchanged AOP probe moves from 21,809 to 11,480 incremental O3 loadable bytes and from 18,513 to 8,632 at Oz. Core moves from 19,317 to 9,004 O3 loadable bytes and from 5,613 to 5,557 DMD templates.
RouteMatcher reuses PathMatcher's concrete variable-map and comparator types. Its null guard keeps the existing exception diagnostic. The paired AOP probe on venus against train tip `a2c47a789` records 1,538 to 1,530 DMD template instances against the 1,537 cap.

Beans exception and definition-default signatures share one nullable string specialization and one nullable Boolean specialization. The paired Security Core probe on venus against train tip `a2c47a789` moves from 2,873 to 2,821 DMD templates against the 2,850 cap. The O3 linked-byte delta moves from 176,591 to 176,519.

### Jackson tree search

The first-match operations check an object's own property and then walk its stored children. A failed path lookup returns a missing node through the existing factory. The plural operations require Java List façades. Both their template counts (B-1) and the application cost of collection methods retained by virtual accumulator overloads (S-1) determine when the plural API can land.

On venus, the pinned `measure_builds.py --framework-sizes --framework jackson-databind` probe measured 4,086 DMD template instances and 418,773 loadable bytes at `26bd3a8c`. First-match search at `755c8bd9` measured 4,088 and 419,469. The experiment with node-valued lists at `13b527ef` measured 5,347 and 446,649. Adding string-valued lists at `f2b34aec` measured 5,702 and 476,197. The module cap is 4,850 template instances.

After `20c53a22` moved `Collection.stream()` out of the vtable, the matched databind probe on venus from `3aae1c61` to `51704b75` and `afa3e76c` measured 3,664 templates at baseline, 3,970 with node-valued searches, and 4,218 with all three plural searches. All fit the existing 4,850 cap. In matched pinned-LDC `flint build --profile size` builds, node-valued searches added 17,056 loadable bytes to gs-rest-service and 16,336 to petclinic-rest. String-valued searches added a further 984 and 976 bytes. The largest new symbols include `ArrayList!JsonNode` dispatch thunks and the class and interface vtables. The node-valued searches stay deferred while the Java class-valued List façade retains that cost in apps that never call tree search. The standalone string-valued search at `b40578b4` against `3aae1c61` adds 1,680 loadable bytes to gs-rest-service and 1,016 to petclinic-rest. The databind probe counted 3,926 templates and 451,873 loadable bytes. Its accumulator uses the Java string List and creates a list only at the first match.

The first-match experiment added 672 bytes of allocated ELF sections at Oz to both applications, from 3,727,853 to 3,728,525 for gs-rest-service and from 10,481,639 to 10,482,311 for petclinic-rest. Both builds used `flint build --profile size` and the pinned LDC on the same matched source trees.

The `hasNonNull` accessors look up a child once and inspect its node type. Tests port the presence assertions from Jackson's ArrayNodeTest and ObjectNodeTest, including the change from explicit null to a value. The same module probe on venus measured 419,469 to 419,989 loadable bytes from `5658e261` to `0f76aad2`, with 4,088 DMD template instances in both builds.

Direct `required` access returns an existing child immediately and formats an index or runtime type name only on failure. The exact exception text distinguishes a wrong container from a missing property or index. From `36d984ed` to `636c7b16`, the jackson-databind probe on venus moved from 419,989 to 420,277 loadable bytes and from 4,088 to 4,096 DMD template instances.
| S-26 | **Accumulate BigInteger products by output column.** Each column combines its limb products and incoming carry in a 96-bit accumulator, then writes one destination word. | On venus with LDC 1.43.0 at `3c6e97ad` the java-math probe moved from 23,764 to 23,668 B O3 and from 21,060 to 21,516 B Oz, in allocated sections. On the earlier matched Java closure with the queued Number bridge, the isolated BigInteger probe moved from 22,844 to 22,748 B O3 and from 20,140 to 20,596 B Oz, under the 22,858 B cap. DMD stayed at 533 templates for the combined package. | Both compilers match Java SE 21 on 4,096 signed multiplication and 4,096 exponentiation cases. Carry-chain and uneven-input tests exercise column boundaries. |
| S-30 | **Validate parameter slots and typed writers before creating a JDBC write plan.** A binder checks the exact entity type once and reuses immutable slot assignments. | The unchanged JDBC probe (2ad6c2d2 against 004a71e5) grew from 5,136 to 5,208 loadable bytes at O3 and from 4,712 to 4,784 at Oz, with 754 to 760 DMD templates. The pinned PostgreSQL fixture exercises reordered prepared slots, SQL NULL rules and DATE/TIMESTAMP writers on DMD and LDC. The opt-in TIMESTAMP leaf packs signed epoch microseconds with checked arithmetic and retains the JDBC round-trip range of ISO years 0001–9999. The pinned PostgreSQL 18.7 fixture and pgjdbc 42.7.14 control agree at the half-microsecond threshold and at the pre-epoch midnight carry. The unchanged probe (b1c948f2 against 0929a527) stayed at 5,208 loadable B at O3 and 4,784 at Oz. DMD templates increased from 760 to 772. The unversioned managed UPDATE plan covers writable fields once and assigns a distinct parameter to the canonical integer key. Resolve the managed target once, skip preparing SQL for a clean entity, and require exactly one updated row. The PostgreSQL fixture checks clean, successful, missing and multi-row outcomes and rolls back the transaction on failure. The unchanged probe (06fabb6e against 4925ec18) grew from 5,508 to 5,548 loadable B at O3 and 5,076 to 5,124 at Oz, with 876 to 885 DMD templates. The versioned plan reserves separate slots for loaded and incremented BIGINT versions. The same identity lookup supplies the loaded version, and dirty checking excludes the provider-owned version. The pinned fixture checks a successful increment, rollback, a distinct connection's committed update causing a stale conflict, and exhaustion before SQL. The unchanged probe (ecd3346f against a3858a64) stayed at 5,548 loadable bytes at O3 and 5,124 at Oz. DMD templates increased from 885 to 894. The ordered batch checks every plan index and its scratch requirements before SQL, then reuses one set of buffers and the caller's held connection. The PostgreSQL fixture checks preflight rejection, a failure after one write, and rollback of the written and pending entries. The unchanged probe (d027a6718 against e826325fa) measured 5,548 loadable bytes at O3 and 5,124 at Oz in both builds. DMD templates increased from 899 to 904. Managed DELETE binds the canonical key and optional loaded BIGINT version from a checked context slot. It retires the row after one affected row and successful statement close. The pinned PostgreSQL fixture checks stale, multi-row and driver failures, and reload after rollback. The unchanged probe (53d30ba44 against ed4ca2f7b) grew from 5,548 to 5,596 loadable bytes at O3 and 5,124 to 5,164 at Oz. DMD templates increased from 904 to 908. Assigned-ID INSERT reserves an invisible identity and reuses the captured values for JDBC and the initial baseline. Exact row count and statement close publish the identity. A capture, bind or SQL error releases the reservation. The unchanged probe (c5dd43481 against ece7d4b03) measured 5,596 loadable bytes at O3 and 5,164 at Oz in both builds, and DMD templates increased from 908 to 919. The pinned fixture checks reuse after failures and rollback after publication. IDENTITY INSERT reserves bounded identity capacity and collects non-key values before JDBC. A provider-owned one-column name vector requests the exact generated key. One integer key and statement close precede writing the ID and activating the captured baseline. Pinned PostgreSQL proves full-context rejection before SQL and rollback on snapshot exhaustion after SQL. The same probe (7cd69fe53 against 36fd67031) measured 5,596 loadable bytes at O3 and 5,164 at Oz in both builds, with DMD templates increasing from 919 to 920. The mixed flush preflights update and delete plan tables, then runs updates followed by deletes on the same held connection. The PostgreSQL fixture proves invalid delete preflight prevents earlier SQL, later delete failure rolls back earlier work, and delete uses the newly published version after an update. The same probe (0638beec8 against 111b3d832) measured 5,596 loadable bytes at O3 and 5,164 at Oz in both builds. DMD templates increased from 920 to 936. Scheduled INSERT actions index scanned roots and checked plans. One scratch set and held connection serve inserts, updates and deletes. Preflight rejects a bad later plan before inserts start. The PostgreSQL fixture checks visibility on the held connection, rollback after later failure and reuse of the IDENTITY key-name buffer. The matched probe (da9657e52 against db706fe89) kept 5,596 loadable bytes at O3 and 5,164 at Oz, with DMD templates moving from 936 to 948. Check the original insert ticket after generated field capture and after binding before executeUpdate. The linked PostgreSQL fixture clears that ticket at capture, bind and close for assigned and generated IDs. Capture rejection allocates zero GC bytes. Both paths retain caller rollback after SQL and can retry with cleared scratch. Matched engine probes (`eaf5ac9e7` against `fd1dd55ec`) measured 5,596 loadable bytes at O3, 5,164 at Oz, and 1,005 DMD templates in both builds. | Module size and template gate, invalid-slot and missing-writer tests, and the linked PostgreSQL fixture. |

| S-164 | **Map Java's byte-array checksum updates to the D slice form.** Keep the whole-array call on the interface; Java's ranged overload is the same D call on a slice. A null slice is empty under R26, and Adler32 and CRC32 reuse one native buffer call while CRC32C walks its table. | On venus the checksum probe moves from 1,320 to 1,512 loadable bytes at Oz (+192) and stays at 320 at O3; it uses 30 DMD templates. The other java-zip leaf probes are unchanged. | java-zip leaf budgets, the checksum array tests on DMD and LDC, and the link-closure audit. |
| S-120 | **Separate archive ownership from the ThreadLocal factory.** Keep native thread ownership in its own dependency-free archive and instantiate array growth in the ThreadLocal binding caller. The fiber installer reserves its slot without linking the native lifecycle. One cold helper serves the factory's null checks. | Stock O3 and Oz Java probes pass after moving native thread ownership out of the Java archive. Before isolation, batch LDC pulled the native startup and exit hooks into 50 unrelated probe/profile pairs through a D runtime string helper. Matched standalone native storage adds 2,416 O3 and 2,512 Oz loadable bytes with 27 joint templates. The fiber installer adds 1,832 O3 and 1,920 Oz bytes over raw FiberLocal with 289 joint templates. Matched LDC release startup installation adds 1,504 bytes to gs-rest-service and 1,528 to petclinic-rest. | Stock Java, java-sys and java-fiber link-closure audits, matched package probes, DMD and LDC factory and interleaved FiberRoute checks, and native detach tests under AddressSanitizer. |

An interface module imports only what its signatures name. A template body's dependency is reached through `imported!` in that body, so a client that imports only the interface does not compile and link the dependency. `flint.beans.factory` now reaches `AnnotationAwareOrderComparator` only inside `ObjectProvider.orderedStream`. On venus, sol-boot's release-flag probe (LDC `-O3 -release -fno-moduleinfo-localclasses -i -L--gc-sections`, a ConditionMessage-only executable plus one unused `BeanFactory` import, base `229ba5ec`) measured the import's cost at 11,648 loadable bytes before and 10,144 after (−1,504). The remainder is not code: gc-sections drops the `flint.java.character` tables and `AntPathMatcher`. It is 7.7 KB of string literals (MIME types, placeholder and charset messages, class names) from the modules `-i` compiles into the one probe object, whose mergeable string sections the linker keeps whole. The canonical gs-rest-service build, with one object per package, measured 3,630,437 to 3,630,421 bytes for the same import. Read a probe's growth by section (`readelf -WS`) before moving more imports: `.rodata.str1.*` growth is this artifact.

Jackson core’s JsonPointer reuses the original expression for segment suffixes and allocates a decoded buffer only for a property with an escape. A pure buffer builder lets D publish that buffer as an immutable string. Compile and the existing stream-write constraint use one interim argument-exception factory. On venus at `5be6db90` and `d8eeff6a`, the standard jackson-core probe stayed at 43,045 loadable bytes and moved from 581 to 600 DMD templates, at its existing cap. The probe exercises the prior streaming API.

Tree pointer traversal shares one iterative walk for `at` and `requiredAt`. The walk keeps the unmatched suffix for the exception and leaves the original pointer reusable. The R21 overloads run the same required-value checks before casting. The matched databind probe on venus from `30c4e089` to `7cf148a5` moved from 420,445 to 426,685 loadable bytes and from 4,104 to 4,114 DMD templates. For the whole batch from `3561ba4e` to `7cf148a5`, pinned LDC with `flint build --profile size` moved gs-rest-service from 3,717,189 to 3,725,117 allocated ELF bytes (+7,928) and petclinic-rest from 10,469,759 to 10,476,967 (+7,208).
