| S-32 | **Take web-http's modules off the Phobos-cascade trigger list (S-9): no direct Phobos call where a Flint helper exists.** Each of these made a Flint object the reason a Phobos archive member is linked. engine-servlet sized its reactor from `std.parallelism.totalCPUs` (its lazily initialized constant is defined only in `parallelism.o`); it now asks engine-web's `availableCores()`, the affinity mask counted the way arena's mark-thread count does. flint.http encoded Basic credentials with `std.base64`; a twenty-line RFC 4648 encoder replaces it. `javaDoubleToString` (MediaType quality values) printed with `std.format`'s `%.*f` and `%.17g`, which linked Phobos's float printer and `std.math.pow` from `flint.http.converter.json.o`; engine-aot's new `fixed` and `general` (C's `%.*f` and `%.*g`, beside `scientific`) print the same text. framework-test's `flint.mock.web` (MockHttpServletRequest and Response) parsed and printed header numbers with `std.conv` and matched text with `std.string.strip` and `std.uni.toLower`; it now uses engine-aot's `parseLong`, `parseDouble` and `decimal`, an ASCII trim and its own case-insensitive compare, and a malformed number throws Spring's `NumberFormatException` instead of `ConvException`. framework/web's remaining `std.conv` uses (data binding's `ConvException` signal and `to!bool`'s `std.uni.toLower`, a parameter index in a message, CORS's and `UriComponents`' ports) go through engine-aot's `decimal` and `parseLong` and Flint's `NumberFormatException`; `getPort` reports bad text with Spring's `IllegalStateException("The port must be an integer: ...")`. | gs-rest-service and petclinic-rest Oz (`flint build --profile size`, linker map) on venus at 4b345b4c: `parallelism.o` is no longer pulled by `flint.engine.servlet.o`. Bytes do not fall yet: `curl.o` still pulls `parallelism.o` while other modules' S-9 triggers remain, and the affinity count is new code (gs-rest 5,617,744 to 5,619,000, petclinic 14,522,312 to 14,523,576); without `base64.o`, gs-rest 5,618,456 and petclinic 14,523,616; without the float printer's instances, gs-rest 5,617,888 and petclinic 14,523,072, and no web-http object references a Phobos member. framework-test probe (DoD budgets, loadable bytes, venus): 1,732,342 to 1,724,074; the probe's map no longer names `flint.mock.web.o` as a Phobos referrer, and the cap (1,552,384) still waits on the next ones (`flint.web.bind.support.o`, `flint.test.web.servlet.request.o`, `flint.engine.aot.expression.o`). Without web's `std.conv`: gs-rest Oz 5,242,952 to 5,243,104 and petclinic 9,545,024 to 9,545,032 (venus, main at e4925ca8's base); neither app's map names a framework/web object as a Phobos referrer any more (boot, arena, engine-aot's expression.d and jdbc.datasource remain); framework-test probe 1,724,074 to 1,711,970. | engine-web `tests/available_cores.d` (all CPUs, then one on a narrowed thread); engine-servlet's `cores 0` unittest; flint.http's base64 unittest (RFC 4648 vectors, Spring's Basic example, ISO-8859-1); `javaDoubleToString`'s unittest (shortest fixed digits, the %.17g fallback); engine-aot's `formatF`/`formatGPrecision` and `fixed`/`general` unittests, positive and negative; framework-test `tests/mock_servlet_header_text_tests.d` (int and length headers, malformed and wide values, request URL ports, Accept-Language weights and trimming, charset case); web `tests/uri_port_text_tests.d` and `default_data_binder_factory_tests.d`'s boolean words and failure cause |
| S-107 | **Assign a whole `Nullable!T(value)`, not `nullable = value`, in modules on an application's link.** `std.typecons.Nullable.opAssign(T)` is a template whose body imports `std.algorithm.mutation`, so every module that instantiates it lists that module in its ModuleInfo, and the reference makes the link take Phobos mutation.o (whose own ModuleInfo imports follow). `flint.jdbc.datasource` did it twice (`supportsSavepoints`, `prepareConnectionForTransaction`) and was petclinic's first referrer of mutation.o. Struct assignment of a constructed `Nullable` is the compiler's identity copy and instantiates nothing. | `ldc2 -output-ll` over the jdbc package: no `std` ModuleInfo reference remains (it was `std.algorithm.mutation`, beside `std.typecons.Nullable!int/bool.opAssign`). petclinic Oz map: jdbc.datasource leaves the first-referrer list and no Flint object pulls mutation.o; O3 10,847,529 unchanged, Oz 10,434,273 to 10,434,241 loadable B. Tests: jdbc on DMD and LDC (32 modules); petclinic allocation gates on LDC and DMD with static verification on and off. | A linker map (`-L-Map`) and the module's `-output-ll` ModuleInfo initializer |
| S-144 | **Make a method that names another package's type a `()` template when most users of its class never call it.** `ByteBuffer.asShortBuffer` through `asDoubleBuffer` call each numeric buffer's `ofBytes`. As `final` methods their bodies are compiled into `flint.java.nio.bytebuffer.o`, which every ByteBuffer user links (for `allocate`), so the reference pulled all five numeric-buffer objects into every ByteBuffer link and ThinLTO left +304 B of their metadata behind. With an empty template parameter list (`ShortBuffer asShortBuffer()() pure`) the body is emitted only in a caller's object; the module-level imports for the return types cost nothing. Templated members are never virtual, so `final` goes (D-Scanner flags it). | java-nio-buffers on venus (v4.2, O3) at e660d4c0f vs the views slice: with `final` methods the package probe went 24,564 → 24,868 B and the `heap-cursor` leaf link gained the five numeric-buffer archive members; with `()` templates the probe is 24,564 B (unchanged) and the leaf's loadable bytes equal the pre-views link (354,753 O3), templates 571. | `framework/java-nio-buffers/tests/byte_numeric_views_test.d` and the module's budgets and link-closure steps |

The string `requiredAt` overload follows S-1 by keeping optional pointer compilation out of the node vtable (D-JACKSON-11). The protected `_at` traversal hook remains virtual. From `1cdb6ad3` to `5dd3cca0`, pinned LDC with `flint build --profile size` on venus reduced allocated ELF sections from 3,725,133 to 3,720,253 bytes in gs-rest-service and from 10,476,967 to 10,472,783 in petclinic-rest. Sharing the out-of-line required-access and typed-cast exception paths at `bdf38451` reduced those sizes to 3,719,741 and 10,472,255 bytes. Call sites still supply the original diagnostic and source location. The databind probe moved from 426,685 loadable bytes to 421,325 with 4,112 DMD template instances.

Jackson pointer prefixes keep slices of the original expression and reuse decoded property names. A parsed segment publishes its cached prefix with an acquire load and release store. Length and hashing preserve Java UTF-16 units. Reusing the segment allocator and a sized buffer for failure diagnostics replaces duplicated construction and string concatenation templates. The matched jackson-core probe on venus at `78ab8174` and `c7466e4a` moved from 43,045 to 43,157 loadable bytes and from 600 to 599 DMD templates. Pinned LDC with `flint build --profile size` added 560 loadable bytes to gs-rest-service and 536 to petclinic-rest.

Jackson pointer append reuses the shared empty pointer and composes the original expressions. Property append counts escaping before allocating its output buffer, then encodes slashes and tildes in a single copy. Index append reuses the integer formatter. On venus, the matched jackson-core probe at `23ab83ed` and `9f2ebed7` measured 43,157 loadable bytes and 599 DMD templates on both sides. In paired pinned-LDC `flint build --profile size` links, gs-rest-service moved from 3,681,631 to 3,681,615 allocated ELF bytes and petclinic-rest moved from 10,401,525 to 10,401,557.

Stream contexts build typed JSON Pointers on demand by walking the parent chain and writing escaped segments from the end of a sized buffer. Interner misses retain an empty name as a static string and hits keep their existing lookup. D-JACKSON-11 makes both context wrappers final so ordinary app links can discard the pointer code. The matched jackson-core probe on venus at `75af5f40` and `08590a56` moved from 43,157 to 43,229 loadable bytes and 599 to 596 DMD templates. In pinned-LDC `flint build --profile size` links on venus, virtual accessors added 7,608 bytes to gs-rest-service and 7,568 to petclinic-rest. The final accessors added 120 and 128 bytes.
The Spring Beans factory exception types reuse the existing `BeanCreationException` and `FatalBeanException` constructors for bean names, diagnostic text and causes. The Beans probe on venus against `68fedff0c` kept 257,478 loadable bytes and moved from 1,632 to 1,642 DMD template instances against the 3,000 cap.
`FiberLocal` lives in the leaf `flint.engine.reactor.fiber_local`, which imports only `core.atomic`, `flint.arena` and the reactor's cell helpers. A module that keeps per-request state imports the leaf, not `flint.engine.reactor`: that package module compiles the reactor's loop, sockets and offload pool, whose `conc` and offload sleeps call `core.time.dur!"usecs"` and `dur!"nsecs"` at run time and so pull Phobos `socket.o` (S-9). The reactor installs a per-thread task-cell resolver while a worker runs its loop, and `flint.engine.reactor` still re-exports `FiberLocal`. On venus, an LDC `-O3 -release -i --gc-sections` probe over an empty program (422,761 loadable bytes) measured an unused `flint.engine.reactor : FiberLocal` import at 945,325 bytes, the leaf import at 423,201 (+440), and a leaf `set`/`get` at 424,313 (+1,552). Context's locale holder, tx's two holders and the slf4j MDC import the leaf.
`FiberLocal` lives in the leaf `flint.engine.reactor.local`, which imports only `core.atomic`, `flint.arena` and the reactor's cell helpers. A module that keeps per-request state imports the leaf, not `flint.engine.reactor`: that package module compiles the reactor's loop, sockets and offload pool, whose `conc` and offload sleeps call `core.time.dur!"usecs"` and `dur!"nsecs"` at run time and so pull Phobos `socket.o` (S-9). The reactor installs a per-thread task-cell resolver while a worker runs its loop, and `flint.engine.reactor` still re-exports `FiberLocal`. On venus, an LDC `-O3 -release -i --gc-sections` probe over an empty program (422,761 loadable bytes) measured an unused `flint.engine.reactor : FiberLocal` import at 945,325 bytes, the leaf import at 423,201 (+440), and a leaf `set`/`get` at 424,313 (+1,552). The leaf's file name matters too (S-19): LDC compiles a DUB package in one invocation and puts the whole package's template instances and compiler-generated helpers in the object of the first source file. Named `fiber_local.d`, the leaf sorted first, defined 305 symbols and linked 18 reactor objects plus conc and arena. Named `local.d`, it sorts after `internal/`, defines 8 symbols and links only itself and `sys/locals`. sol-aop's `aop` framework probe (Oz, with ExposeInvocationInterceptor on the leaf) went from 22,072 to 8,688 incremental loadable bytes. A leaf meant to stay light must not be the first source file of its package.

**Represent Java NIO byte order as a value.** A private boolean distinguishes both named constants and the target endian selects the native order at compile time. On venus the matched Java NIO probe at `0ee09533d` added 72 loadable bytes at O3 and Oz with one DMD template instance. The new-module-rule caps are 80 bytes and 2 instances. The stock closure probe added 120 bytes in each profile with zero closure violations.

**Reuse the canonical RuntimeException for relative buffer underflow.** The leaf adds a zero-argument subclass and inherits message and cause handling. The matched standalone probe at `ff9a41a2f` added 640 O3 and 640 Oz loadable bytes over a RuntimeException control with 436 DMD instances on venus. The jdk-leaf-rule caps are 704 bytes and 480 instances. The stock underflow probe retains nine known aggregate module records in each profile, tracked by the new-probe ratchet. The independent ByteOrder probe retains none.

**Reuse the canonical RuntimeException for relative buffer overflow.** The leaf adds a zero-argument subclass and inherits message and cause handling. The matched standalone probe at `3d29a30b1` added 624 loadable bytes at O3 and Oz over the RuntimeException control, with 436 DMD instances on venus. The jdk-leaf-rule caps are 687 bytes and 480 instances. Independent stock probes cover the exception and the existing byte order and underflow paths.

**Reuse the canonical IllegalStateException for buffer reset without a mark.** The leaf adds a zero-argument subclass and inherits message and cause handling. The matched standalone probe at `fa49a6041` added 624 loadable bytes at O3 and Oz over the IllegalStateException control, with 436 DMD instances on venus. The jdk-leaf-rule caps are 687 bytes and 480 instances. The package benchmark exercises the mark, underflow and overflow exceptions through one handler.

**Reuse the canonical UnsupportedOperationException for writes to read-only buffers.** The leaf adds a zero-argument subclass and inherits message and cause handling. The matched standalone probe at `d1eb6b57f` added 640 O3 and 640 Oz loadable bytes over the UnsupportedOperationException control, with 436 DMD instances on venus. The jdk-leaf-rule caps are 704 bytes and 480 instances. The package benchmark exercises all the buffer exceptions through one handler.

**Keep heap buffers outside the Java NIO exception archive.** The heap cursor and views share backing storage and use the separate java-nio-buffers package. The matched probe at `b514499f4` added 20,324 O3 and 18,508 Oz loadable bytes with 572 DMD instances on venus. The five existing NIO probe reports match their previous stock results exactly. The concrete heap probe covers the abstract Buffer and tracks only the known flint.java.lang module records. The four bulk read overloads advance relative cursors after copying and keep absolute cursors fixed. The matched probe at `03518b401` measures 22,124 O3 and 20,276 Oz bytes with 573 DMD instances. The initial bulk read implementation measured 23,084 and 20,756 bytes. Keeping the shared overlap-safe copy and the cold range and array-size diagnostics out of line keeps the package within its existing caps. Bulk array writes add the relative and absolute overloads, with the same overlap handling. Their first matched build measures 23,180 O3 and 21,060 Oz bytes with 574 DMD instances. Sharing the cold exception construction and slicing both arrays inside the common transfer keeps one copy of that code. The matched candidate at `f5270054e` measures 21,996 O3 and 20,180 Oz bytes with 572 instances, down from the bulk-read base of 22,124 and 20,276 bytes with 573 instances. Buffer-to-buffer transfers and compaction reuse the same checked copy. The initial matched build measures 23,028 O3 and 21,196 Oz bytes with 573 DMD instances. Sharing the null exception path and heap offsets, and directly advancing validated cursors without changing their marks, reduces the measured candidate at `8ea4a252b` to 22,524 O3 and 20,692 Oz bytes with 573 instances. The coordinator approved the 167-byte cap increase at the measured O3 size. The scalar indexed read and write now report Java's no-detail index exception. Sharing the width check and cold exception takes the matched probe from 22,524 O3 and 20,692 Oz bytes with 573 DMD instances to 22,188 and 20,356 bytes with 572 instances. The four ordered short overloads share checked unsigned byte assembly and storage across relative and absolute access. The first short build measured 23,532 O3 and 21,700 Oz bytes with 574 DMD instances on the original base. With the shared width check and out-of-line two-byte load and store, the matched candidate at `ab895a807` measures 22,884 O3 and 21,052 Oz bytes with 572 instances, 696 bytes over the corrected scalar baseline in both profiles. The coordinator approved a 360-byte cap increase. The remaining 20 ordered primitive overloads share width validation and direct byte loads and stores with short. The first matched candidate measured 24,668 O3 and 22,836 Oz bytes with 574 DMD instances. Reversing only the occupied bytes through the byte-swap intrinsic reduces the matched candidate at `4d0cb5ab8` to 24,436 O3 and 22,604 Oz bytes with 574 instances. The approved cap increases by 1,552 bytes over the previous 22,884 O3 and 21,052 Oz bytes, with the template cap unchanged. The heap character buffer shares the lower archive with the abstract Buffer and cursor. Its same-archive prototype retained ByteBuffer metadata because LDC emitted ByteBuffer cast templates in the first object. The isolated character probe retains only the known Java aggregate module records. The matched character workload measures 16,556 O3 and 14,724 Oz bytes with 509 DMD instances. Moving the shared source leaves the ByteBuffer fixture and its source intact and changes its matched result from 24,436 O3 and 22,604 Oz bytes with 574 instances to 24,460 and 22,620 bytes with 559 instances. Heap byte-to-character views retain a single backing slice and capture the parent's byte order. The packed backing tag preserves the 53-byte CharBuffer instance. A virtual factory retains CharBuffer code in the ordinary ByteBuffer probe and measures 30,348 O3 and 28,076 Oz bytes. The final factory keeps the delegate form and demand-links its code. The matched ByteBuffer package at `589c215ad` measures 24,564 O3 and 22,724 Oz bytes with 559 DMD instances. The leaf measures 24,628 and 22,788 bytes with 556 instances. The lower backing change accounts for 48 bytes and the factory's module records for 56. The matched CharBuffer package measures 18,028 O3 and 16,036 Oz bytes with 523 instances, and its leaf measures 18,108 and 16,116 bytes with 507 instances within the existing caps.

**Copy heap character words once, in the overlap direction.** Bulk array reads and writes validate their windows before calling one allocation-free transfer. Disjoint native-order ranges use a slice copy. Aliased or swapped ranges share a loop that reads both bytes before writing either, so a byte-backed view can safely overlap the same character array at an odd origin. On venus the unchanged CharBuffer package probe measured 18,028 O3 and 16,036 Oz loadable bytes with 523 DMD instances before the eight overloads. The initial implementation measured 20,876 and 18,876 with 525 instances. The common copy loop and out-of-line exception paths brought it to 20,284 and 18,348 with 523 instances. The enforced leaf probe changed from 18,108 and 16,116 to 20,364 and 18,428, with 507 instances in both runs. The linked ByteBuffer package and leaf probes retained their byte and template counts.

**Reuse the character copy for buffer transfers and compaction.** Each put validates both cursor windows before moving the code units. Relative puts advance both cursors after the copy, and compact clears the mark without allocating another view. The unchanged CharBuffer package probe on venus rose from 20,284 O3 and 18,348 Oz loadable bytes with 523 DMD instances to 21,988 and 20,020 bytes with 526 instances. The enforced leaf rose from 20,364 O3 and 18,428 Oz bytes with 507 instances to 22,068 and 20,100 bytes with 510. Sharing the read-only exception constructor in an additional helper cost 32 bytes in both profiles, so the measured port retains the existing exception path. Both ByteBuffer probes retain their byte and template counts.

**Compare character windows through one code-unit scan.** CharBuffer compares unsigned remaining units across native and byte-backed storage, and hashes the same window backwards. Equality, ordering and mismatch share an allocation-free scan. The unchanged CharBuffer package probe on venus moved from 21,988 O3 and 20,020 Oz loadable bytes with 526 DMD instances to 23,932 and 21,964 bytes with 531. The enforced leaf moved from 22,068 O3 and 20,100 Oz bytes with 510 instances to 24,012 and 22,044 with 515. A scan indexing both validated byte windows gave identical O3 size and cost 8 more bytes at Oz, so the port reuses the scalar code-unit load. The ByteBuffer probes retained their linked sizes. Their two added DMD instances take the package to 561 and the enforced leaf to 558, within the existing 630 caps.

**Keep the original character-buffer window when forming a subsequence.** Relative reads use the existing scalar load and bare bounds check. A subsequence allocates one user-visible view with the same capacity and storage, an independent cursor, and a fresh mark. Invalid endpoints use the shared stack formatter and Java's start/end diagnostic. The unchanged CharBuffer package probe on venus moved from 23,932 O3 and 21,964 Oz loadable bytes with 531 DMD instances to 24,612 and 22,652 with 534. The enforced leaf moved from 24,012 O3 and 22,044 Oz bytes with 515 instances to 24,692 and 22,732 with 516. Both ByteBuffer probes retain their linked sizes and template counts.

**Reuse relative put for character-buffer reads.** CharBuffer reads transfer into the caller's view through the existing overlap and byte-order copy, with the source limit restored by a scope guard. EOF and full destinations return before copying. On venus the unchanged CharBuffer package probe moved from 24,612 O3 and 22,652 Oz loadable bytes to 24,860 and 22,900, with 534 DMD instances in both runs. The enforced leaf moved from 24,692 O3 and 22,732 Oz bytes to 24,940 and 22,980, with 516 instances in both runs. Both ByteBuffer probes retain their linked sizes and template counts.
Assigning a raw value to a `std.typecons.Nullable` (`nullable = value`) calls `Nullable.opAssign(T)`, which instantiates `std.algorithm.mutation.move`/`moveEmplace`; the link then takes them from Phobos's `mutation.o`, whose references reach `curl.o` and the network cascade (S-9). Construct and assign the whole value instead (`nullable = Nullable!bool(value)`): the copyable constructor plus struct assignment instantiates nothing from `std.algorithm`. `BeanDefinitionDefaults.setLazyInit` did this, and sol-context's key-only cache probe measured 474,377 → 1,000,661 O3 ELF load bytes from it; DMD `-vtemplates` over beans showed four `std.algorithm.mutation` instances before the fix and none after.

Jackson's `nextIntValue` and `nextLongValue` advance one token through `nextToken` and reuse the existing integer getters, including their overflow checks. The matched jackson-core probe on venus at `00668089` and `cf8b0071` measured 43,229 loadable bytes and 596 DMD template instances on both sides. With pinned LDC and `flint build --profile size`, gs-rest-service moved from 3,681,735 to 3,681,983 allocated ELF bytes and petclinic-rest moved from 10,401,645 to 10,401,925.

Jackson's `nextBooleanValue` advances one token and returns `Nullable!bool` (R5) to distinguish a Boolean false from any other token. The matched jackson-core probe on venus at `cf8b0071` and `28a0ab30` measured 43,229 loadable bytes on both sides and 596 to 657 DMD template instances. The API-growth rule raises the cap from 600 to 670 to cover the 61 new instances plus headroom. Pinned LDC with `flint build --profile size` moved gs-rest-service from 3,681,983 to 3,682,063 allocated ELF bytes and petclinic-rest from 10,401,925 to 10,401,957.

Jackson checks the current token for `isExpectedNumberIntToken` and reports `NumberTypeFP.UNKNOWN` for textual JSON. The accessors read the metadata without advancing the parser or decoding the value. The matched jackson-core probe on venus at `3eccea53` and `477af524` measured 43,229 loadable bytes and 657 DMD template instances on both sides. Pinned LDC with `flint build --profile size` moved gs-rest-service from 3,682,079 to 3,682,223 allocated ELF bytes and petclinic-rest from 10,401,989 to 10,402,157.

Jackson's native object and type ID methods report the format's capabilities and use `Any` (R23) for ID values. Plain JSON returns false for the capability queries and uses a shared exception path for native ID writes. On venus at `c60c8f6b` and `98a7f2f5`, the matched jackson-core probe moved from 43,229 to 43,917 loadable bytes and from 657 to 658 DMD templates. With pinned LDC and `flint build --profile size`, gs-rest-service moved from 3,682,383 to 3,683,335 allocated ELF bytes and petclinic-rest moved from 10,403,421 to 10,404,357.

Jackson's default `writeOmittedProperty` returns its generator without touching the write context. On venus at `e8097ec65` and `dba1e28b4`, the matched jackson-core probe moved from 43,917 to 44,037 loadable bytes and stayed at 658 DMD template instances. With pinned LDC and `flint build --profile size`, gs-rest-service moved from 3,683,367 to 3,683,503 allocated ELF bytes and petclinic-rest moved from 10,404,357 to 10,404,509.

Jackson's `JsonFactory.copy` creates a factory from the original's configuration. The copy starts with its own lazy name table and becomes independent on first use. The matched jackson-core probe on venus at `dba1e28b4` and `4a2a41311` stayed at 44,037 loadable bytes and moved from 658 to 659 DMD template instances. With pinned LDC and `flint build --profile size`, gs-rest-service moved from 3,683,503 to 3,683,487 allocated ELF bytes and petclinic-rest moved from 10,404,509 to 10,404,541.

Jackson's UTF-8 parser and generator expose stream feature masks directly from their factory. Custom stream implementations can derive the same mask from their existing `isEnabled` method. The matched jackson-core probe on venus at `4a2a41311` and `e56d436ce` moved from 44,037 to 44,109 loadable bytes and stayed at 659 DMD template instances. Pinned LDC with `flint build --profile size` moved gs-rest-service from 3,683,487 to 3,683,607 allocated ELF bytes and petclinic-rest from 10,404,541 to 10,404,597.

Jackson's `writePropertyId` renders a signed decimal ID using the existing numeric writer and passes one owned name to `writeName`. The matched jackson-core probe on venus at `e56d436ce` and `8ff0d3ae8` moved from 44,109 to 44,389 loadable bytes and from 659 to 660 DMD template instances. Pinned LDC with `flint build --profile size` moved gs-rest-service from 3,683,607 to 3,684,095 allocated ELF bytes and petclinic-rest from 10,404,597 to 10,405,085.
### Jackson feature masks

The six UFCS helpers use the pinned Java ordinal shifts with signed 32-bit masks for the serialization and deserialization enums and signed 64-bit masks for MapperFeature. The existing default routines still supply mapper startup configuration. The 76-case Jackson 3.1.8 fixture covers declaration order, default flags, masks and enabledIn on DMD and LDC.

Matched release O3 builds on venus with the same pinned LDC, Flint binary and native providers compared `3f156885b` with `eccebbb7d`. File-backed allocated sections went from 3,879,367 to 3,879,351 bytes for gs-rest-service and from 10,848,189 to 10,848,157 bytes for petclinic-rest. The Databind module probe measured 455,361 to 455,361 incremental loadable bytes and 3,933 to 3,933 DMD template instances.

The instruction probe used 1,000 warmup requests and 2,000 measured requests per route. The last two columns were equal on both builds.

| Request | Instructions per request | Jackson instructions per request | Allocations per request | Bytes per request |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| gs-rest greeting | 19,801.75 to 19,800.935 | 1,783 to 1,783 | 2 | 32 |
| petclinic validation 400 | 103,184.693 to 103,188.658 | 13,652.54 to 13,652.776 | 40 | 3,504 |
| petclinic owner 1 | 215,843.864 to 214,870.07 | 10,873.499 to 10,873.499 | 86 | 7,040 |
**Size the character window before rendering UTF-8.** CharBuffer scans the remaining UTF-16 units for the result size, then writes into that one allocation. The scalar load works across both backing kinds and byte orders, and R14 gives each lone half one U+FFFD. Both compilers match the allocation cost of one same-size array for nonempty output and zero for empty output. On venus the unchanged package probe moved from 24,860 O3 and 22,900 Oz loadable bytes with 534 DMD instances to 25,724 and 23,740 bytes with 538. The enforced leaf moved from 24,940 and 22,980 bytes with 516 instances to 25,804 and 23,820 with 520. The fixed-stack Phobos encoder trial measured 25,868 and 23,900 bytes with 566 package instances, over the 560 template cap. Writing bounded UTF-8 directly keeps template counts under that cap. Starting the reverse loop at the array length and decrementing after the bounds check adds 16 O3 bytes and saves 112 Oz bytes against the initial bounded writer. Both ByteBuffer probes retain their linked sizes and template counts.
**Represent Java NIO CodingErrorAction as an R15 value struct.** A private byte tag (REPORT as tag 0 for `.init`, IGNORE as 1, REPLACE as 2) avoids GC allocation and class-metadata overhead while keeping mutable variable assignment and value equality. On venus against `393f7145e`, the standalone `flint.java.nio.charset.codingerroraction` leaf probe measured 104 loadable bytes at O3 and Oz with 1 DMD template instance over the generic control (down from 584/616 bytes for an immutable-class draft) and zero link-closure findings. The combined `java-nio` package probe moved from 2,708 to 2,796 loadable bytes and from 449 to 450 DMD templates, within the existing 2,862-byte and 494-template caps.
**Share one UTF-16 scan/write loop and out-of-line store for CharBuffer.put(String).** `put(String, int, int)` and final `put(String)` reuse `flint.engine.aot.text.decodeUtf8` (already linked in the baseline) through one 3-argument `scanOrWriteUtf16` helper (using `size_t.max` bounds for the count pass) and an out-of-line `store` shared with scalar puts, allocating zero GC memory. On venus against `e43093694`, the initial separate-loop draft measured 27,140 O3 and 25,148 Oz enforced leaf bytes (520 templates); sharing the loop and out-of-line `store` reduced it by 104 O3 and 96 Oz bytes to 27,036 O3 / 25,052 Oz (package 26,956 O3) with 0 new templates (538 package / 520 leaf). Both ByteBuffer probes stay unchanged at 24,564 package / 24,628 leaf O3 bytes and 561 / 558 templates.
**Keep cold CharBuffer validation helpers out of line.** Marking `checkArray` and `checkWritableHeap` `pragma(inline, false)` alongside `checkWritable` avoids duplicating cold throw blocks across virtual vtable entries, while sharing `putUtf8Range` avoids double-counting UTF-16 length in one-argument `put(scope const(char)[])`. On venus against `bbbb1e0b3`, porting `CharBuffer.append(wchar)` initially measured 27,020 package / 27,100 leaf O3 bytes (25,108 Oz); out-lining `checkArray` and `checkWritableHeap` brought it down by 72 O3 and 96 Oz bytes to **26,948 package / 27,028 leaf O3 bytes and 25,012 Oz bytes** (-8 O3 / -40 Oz net below `bbbb1e0b3`, within unchanged 26,956 / 27,036 caps) with 0 new templates (538 package / 520 leaf). Both ByteBuffer probes stay unchanged at 24,564 package / 24,628 leaf O3 bytes and 561 / 558 templates.
**Keep Java NIO CoderResult in its own `java-nio-charset` package as an R15 value struct.** Representing `CoderResult` with a private `(kind, errorLength)` value struct (`UNDERFLOW` as `.init`), out-of-line `formatError` writing decimal digits into one exact-sized `char[]`, and out-of-line cold exception helpers avoids Java's soft-reference cache and keeps `java-nio`'s existing buffer-exception probes free of LDC first-object cross-talk. On venus against `81d4a5fde`, the standalone `framework/java-nio-charset` package probe measures 1,904 O3 loadable bytes and 440 DMD templates (caps 2,095 / 484), and the enforced `flint.java.nio.charset.coderresult` leaf probe measures 1,936 O3 / 1,560 Oz loadable bytes over `runtime-exception.d` with 440 DMD templates (224 leaf + 216 probe; caps 2,130 / 484) and exact 18 legacy `flint.java.lang` module-record entries in `framework/java/closure-baseline.json`.
**Compute heap-buffer alignment from the array offset and keep its throws in one cold helper.** Every Flint `ByteBuffer` is a heap buffer, so `alignmentOffset` is `(origin + index) & (unitSize - 1)` in long arithmetic (HotSpot's 64-bit byte-array base offset, 16, is a multiple of every allowed unit size), and `alignedSlice` reuses `slice`. Java's three argument errors share one out-of-line `throwAlignment` and one non-templated `labelledValue` message builder in `flint.java.nio.buffer`. On venus (v4.2 toolchain) against the parent `acc154f35`, the `java-nio-buffers` package probe stays at 24,564 incremental linked bytes and its DMD template count rises from 571 to 575 against the 630 cap; `java-nio-char-buffer` stays at 26,948 bytes and rises from 540 to 541 of 560.

**Keep Java NIO core byte-channel interfaces in `framework/java-nio-channels` as template-free leaves.** Splitting `Channel`, `InterruptibleChannel`, `ReadableByteChannel`, `WritableByteChannel`, `ByteChannel`, `ScatteringByteChannel`, `GatheringByteChannel`, and `SeekableByteChannel` into one interface per leaf module avoids LDC first-object cross-talk and keeps `ByteBuffer` implementation code out of every interface leaf probe (`Channel` and `InterruptibleChannel` link only `Closeable` at 219 DMD templates; the six byte-channel leaves count 248 DMD templates). On venus against `716710f9e`, the combined `framework/java-nio-channels` package probe over `Closeable` + `ByteBuffer` measures 5,872 O3 loadable bytes and 495 DMD templates (caps 6,460 / 545), and the eight enforced leaf probes measure 2,012–2,308 O3 and 2,092–4,180 Oz loadable bytes (caps ceil 1.10x) with only the exact 18 inherited legacy `flint.java.lang` module records per probe.
**S-130. Keep `java.lang.annotation` enums and R2 meta-annotations in an optional `framework/java-lang-annotation` package as zero-aggregate leaves.** Keeping `ElementType`, `RetentionPolicy`, `Documented`, `Inherited`, `Native`, `Retention`, `Target`, and `Repeatable` (using only the selective `flint.java.classref.ClassRef` leaf) out of `flint.java.lang` avoids linking the Java aggregate or any runtime reflection/exception hierarchy. On venus against `87ff70165`, the `framework/java-lang-annotation` package probe over `flint.java.classref` measures 64 O3 / 64 Oz incremental loadable bytes and 13 DMD templates (caps 71 B / 15 templates, ceil 1.10x). The eight enforced leaf probes pass stock link-closure with zero violations and zero legacy `flint.java.lang` records: `documented`/`inherited`/`native` 0 O3/Oz B, 2 templates (caps 0/3); `elementtype`/`retentionpolicy` 16 O3/Oz B, 0 templates (caps 18/0); `retention` 16 O3/Oz B, 1 template (caps 18/2); `target` 16 O3/Oz B, 2 templates (caps 18/3); `repeatable` 56 O3/Oz B, 4 templates (caps 62/5).

**S-131. Port `java.lang.Math`'s exact IEEE-754 core as a pure static leaf.** `flint.java.lang.math` implements rounding, `abs`/`max`/`min`, the multiply-high family and the IEEE-recommended functions (`ulp`, `nextAfter`, `scalb` and the rest) with raw bit unions and no Phobos or libm, so every method is `pure nothrow @nogc` and allocates nothing. The venus core probe added 2,024 loadable bytes at O3 and 1,968 at Oz with zero closure violations, and pinned DMD counted 3 templates. The jdk-leaf-rule caps are 2,227 bytes and 4 templates. A 9,838-call differential run against OpenJDK 21.0.13 matched bit for bit on DMD and LDC, at both debug and release optimisation.

**S-132. Make throwing members of a zero-aggregate JDK leaf zero-parameter templates that import their exceptions locally.** `Math`'s exact integer family (`addExact`..`absExact`, `toIntExact`, `floorDiv`/`floorMod`, `ceilDiv`/`ceilMod` and integral `clamp`) throws `ArithmeticException` and `IllegalArgumentException`, which live in the `flint.java.lang` aggregate. A module-level import of those exceptions in `flint.java.lang.math` raised the core probe, which calls none of them, from 2,024 to 3,924 O3 bytes and from 3 to 224 DMD templates, and added 10 undeclared aggregate module records. With `static int addExact()(int, int)` and a function-local `import flint.java.lang : ArithmeticException;` in the cold throw helper, the core probe measures 2,024 O3 / 1,968 Oz bytes and 3 templates again on venus with zero violations. A program that calls the exact family pays for the exception hierarchy: an unregistered probe over all 36 members measured 8,780 O3 / 7,460 Oz bytes and 307 templates, almost all of it the aggregate. Guard Java's `/` and `%` explicitly: a zero divisor throws, and `MIN_VALUE / -1` wraps, where D traps (and LLVM leaves it undefined). Write the divisor as `y | (y == 0)` after the zero check, because DMD `-O -inline` otherwise folds the dead division of a literal zero divisor into a compile error.

**S-145. Port `java.lang.StrictMath` as a thin alias/forwarding leaf over `flint.java.lang.math` and `flint.java.internal.fdlibm`.** Aliasing the 76 identical `Math` overload sets directly (`alias ceil = Math.ceil;`, etc.), implementing only the two `copySign` overloads whose Java 21 NaN-sign contract differs from `Math.copySign` (treating every NaN sign bit as positive), and forwarding the 20 FDLIBM methods through zero-parameter templates that locally import `flint.java.internal.fdlibm` avoids duplicating machine code or template instantiations. On venus against `8e83da9b7` with LDC 1.43.0 and DMD 2.114, the `flint.java.lang.strictmath/core` probe adds 2,064 O3 and 2,008 Oz incremental loadable bytes (+40 B over `flint.java.lang.math/core`), 1 DMD template instance, and zero link-closure violations (`leaves: ["flint.java.lang.math"]`, zero `flint.java.lang` aggregate and zero `fdlibm` symbols). The jdk-leaf-rule caps are 2,271 bytes and 2 templates. A 63,200-call differential run against pinned OpenJDK 21.0.13+9 matched bit for bit on DMD and LDC in debug and release builds.
**S-146. Isolate `java.net` checked exceptions in `framework/java-net-exceptions` so LDC batch compilation does not couple them to form codecs or address parsers.** Under v4.2 batch archives, placing `UnknownHostException` beside `InetAddress` or `URLDecoder` caused LDC to emit shared `RTInfoImpl` and string templates into the alphabetically first module, dragging unrelated `java.net` leaves into exception and codec probes. Putting `flint.java.net.unknownhostexception` in its own `flint:java`-only package (`framework/java-net-exceptions`, following `sol-jdk-core` guidance) isolates the exception hierarchy at the bottom of the `java-net-*` DAG with zero cross-leaf pollution. On venus against `f02fc7a46`, the `framework/java-net-exceptions` package probe measures 3,188 O3 loadable bytes and 436 DMD templates (caps 3,507 B / 480 templates, ceil 1.10x), and the enforced `flint.java.net.unknownhostexception` leaf probe measures 3,204 O3 / 2,020 Oz loadable bytes and 220 DMD templates (caps 3,525 B / 242 templates) with 0 new closure violations (exact 18 legacy `flint.java.lang` records in `framework/java/closure-baseline.json`).

**S-149. Give `java.net.InetAddress` its own `framework/java-net-address` package above `java-net-exceptions`, so its literal parser never shares a batch archive with the form codecs.** Under v4.2 batch archives, `inetaddress.d` sorting before `internal/formcharset.d` in `framework/java-net` pulled 2-7 undeclared `java-net` modules into the `urldecoder` probe. In `framework/java-net-address` (depending on `flint:java`, `flint:engine-aot` and `flint:java-net-exceptions` only; `sun.net.util.IPAddressUtil`'s helpers stay private in `flint.java.net.internal.ipaddressutil`, owned through `also`), the codec probes are untouched and the `flint.java.net.inetaddress` leaf probe carries only the 18 inherited `flint.java.lang` records. On venus under v4.2, the package probe (`getByName` of a literal plus `getHostAddress`) measures 20,360 O3 loadable bytes and 629 DMD templates (caps 22,396 B / 692, ceil 1.10x); the leaf probe (byte-array factories, scoped IPv6, literal `getByName`/`getAllByName`, predicates, equality and text) measures 22,788 O3 / 20,908 Oz loadable bytes and 394 DMD templates (caps 25,067 B / 434).

**S-150. In a zero-aggregate JDK leaf, make a string-returning helper a zero-parameter template so the caller instantiates its array copy.** `Byte.toString(byte)` and `Short.toString(short)` call `flint.java.internal.integers.javaIntText`, which ends in `text[at .. $].idup`. As an ordinary function in the library, its object referred to `object.idup!char` and the linker resolved it from the first archive member that emitted that instance, `flint.java.character.o`, which pulled in `flint.java.lang`, `flint.java.sql`, `flint.java.time` and the `flint.java.util` calendar family. The `flint.java.lang.byte_/core` probe measured 3,660 O3 bytes with nine undeclared module records. With `string javaIntText()(long)` and `static string toString()(byte)`, the probe instantiates the copy itself. On venus against `abd0afd48` with LDC 1.43.0 and DMD 2.114, `flint.java.lang.byte_/core` adds 624 O3 / 720 Oz bytes and `flint.java.lang.short_/core` 784 / 720 bytes, each with zero violations and 58 DMD template instances. Nearly all of those are druntime's allocation hooks: `.idup`, `new char[]` and an array literal all instantiate about 50 (`_d_newarrayT`, `_d_arrayliteralTX`, `__arrayAlloc` and their traits), so any GC string costs that much. The jdk-leaf-rule caps are 792 / 863 bytes and 64 templates. The throwing `parseX`, `valueOf(String)` and `decode` stay zero-parameter templates over the status-returning `nothrow @nogc` parser (S-132). A 66,168-call differential run of `Byte`/`Short` parse and decode against OpenJDK 21.0.13 matched every value and every `NumberFormatException` message on DMD and LDC in debug and release builds, and 12 of 13 parser mutants were killed. The 13th, which accepts overlong UTF-8, is killed by a unit test.
**S-157. Put `java.net.InetSocketAddress` in its own `framework/java-net-socket-address` package on top of `java-net` and `java-net-address`, so the case tables for unresolved host names stay out of the address and codec probes.** `InetSocketAddress` needs `SocketAddress` (in `framework/java-net`) and `InetAddress` (in `framework/java-net-address`). Its unresolved host names compare with `String.equalsIgnoreCase` and hash as `toLowerCase(Locale.ROOT)`, so `flint.java.net.internal.hostcase` (owned through `also`) links six `flint.java.character` tables. `equals` and `hashCode` are virtual overrides through `mixin JavaObject`, so every probe that constructs the class keeps those tables, even when it never compares host names. A separate package keeps them out of the `inetaddress` and `urldecoder` leaves. Depending on `java-net`'s batch archive adds no `java-net` module records and no new batch-placement rows; the leaf carries only the 16 inherited `flint.java.lang` records. On venus under v4.2, the package probe (`(String, int)` with a literal, then `toString`) measures 55,812 O3 loadable bytes and 513 DMD templates (caps 61,394 B / 565, ceil 1.10x). The leaf probe (wildcard, bound and unresolved addresses, `(String, int)`, equality, hash and text) measures 57,272 O3 / 53,800 Oz loadable bytes and 273 DMD templates (caps 63,000 B / 301). In the earlier private map (d3b4a2012), about 27,600 B were `flint.java.character` tables, 16,800 B of them only for the final-sigma word test. This is the first candidate for a cut if a compact cased/word-class table becomes available in `flint:java`.

**S-151. Give the `java.net.spi` resolver contract its own `framework/java-net-spi` package above `java-net-address`.** `InetAddressResolver`, its `LookupPolicy`, and `InetAddressResolverProvider` with its `Configuration` need only `InetAddress` and the `Stream` facade in `flint:java`, so they stay out of the address, socket-address and codec archives. The `lookupByName` signature returns `Stream!InetAddress`, and importing `flint.java.util.stream` costs most of the leaf's DMD template instances: a one-method interface returning `Stream!InetAddress` instantiates 1,634 templates, against 238 when it returns `InetAddress` (DMD `-vtemplates`, same imports otherwise). The machine code stays small. On venus under v4.2, the package probe (`LookupPolicy.of` and its message) measures 6,388 O3 loadable bytes and 2,047 DMD templates (caps 7,027 B / 2,252, ceil 1.10x). The `flint.java.net.spi.inetaddressresolver` leaf probe measures 6,340 O3 / 4,964 Oz bytes and 1,638 templates (caps 6,974 B / 1,802), with `InetAddress`'s module records declared and the 18 inherited `flint.java.lang` records. The `flint.java.net.spi.inetaddressresolverprovider` probe (a subclass) measures 1,000 O3 / 1,000 Oz bytes and 411 templates (caps 1,100 B / 453), with no violations. A lighter `Stream` declaration in `flint:java` would cut the template cost.

**S-158. Give `java.net.DatagramPacket` its own `framework/java-net-datagram` package on top of `java-net-socket-address`, and expect its leaf to carry `InetSocketAddress`.** `getSocketAddress` returns a new `InetSocketAddress`, and the class's virtual `toString`, `equals` and `hashCode` keep `flint.java.net.internal.hostcase` and its six `flint.java.character` tables in every probe that calls it. The package probe, which only builds a packet and sets its length, measures 9,072 O3 loadable bytes and 537 DMD templates on venus under v4.2 (caps 9,980 B / 591, ceil 1.10x). The `flint.java.net.datagrampacket` leaf probe (the address and socket-address constructors, `setLength`, `setPort` and `getSocketAddress`) measures 51,644 O3 / 48,384 Oz loadable bytes and 300 DMD templates (caps 56,809 B / 330). Like S-157, it carries only the 16 inherited `flint.java.lang` records and adds no `java-net` module records. A compact case table in `flint:java` (S-157) would shrink this leaf by the same amount.

**S-152. A zero-aggregate JDK leaf's non-template functions must not instantiate a template declared in a shared internal module.** `Integer.compress(int, int)` and `expand` first called `compressBits!uint` and `expandBits!uint` from `flint.java.internal.integers`. They are ordinary functions, so their instances were compiled into the library, and the linker resolved `compressBits!uint` from `flint.java.internal.integers.o`. That object linked its own module record and `flint.java.lang.character.decimaldigits`, the parser's Unicode digit table. The `flint.java.lang.integer/core` probe measured 1,712 O3 / 1,392 Oz bytes with two undeclared module records, and `flint.java.lang.long_/core` 2,576 / 1,472. Each leaf now keeps its own eight-line loop, and the shared digit formatter `formatJavaDigits` is a zero-parameter template, as S-150 describes for `javaIntText`. On venus against `b031ee72f` with LDC 1.43.0 and DMD 2.114, the probes add 1,600 / 1,296 bytes (Integer) and 2,464 / 1,360 bytes (Long), each with zero violations and 63 DMD template instances. The jdk-leaf-rule caps are 1,760 / 2,711 bytes and 70 templates. A template whose own callers are templates, such as `unsignedQuotient` under `divideUnsigned()()`, is safe, because the probe instantiates it. A 270,515-call differential run against OpenJDK 21.0.13 matched every value and message on DMD and LDC, debug and release, except one case that R14 sets: when long `parseUnsignedLong` text ends in a supplementary character, Java's error text keeps a lone high surrogate and Flint writes U+FFFD. Of 24 mutants, the run killed 20; the other four are equivalent or dead code (receipts `.flint/sol-framework/integer-long/mutants.txt`).
**S-153. Give `java.net.Proxy` its own `framework/java-net-proxy` package, because joining `java-net-socket-address` made it a first-object passenger of the `InetSocketAddress` probe.** Under v4.2 batch scope, LDC emits every template instance of the batch into the first-sorted root object. With `proxy.d` in `java-net-socket-address`, `inetsocketaddress.o` referred to `Proxy`'s `__Class`, so the `flint.java.net.inetsocketaddress` probe linked `proxy.o`. That added the `flint.java.net.proxy` module record (+40 B, 57,272 to 57,312 O3) and two violations. A `.text`/`__Class` trigger like this one is not something the closure gate's first-object allowances can express. In its own package (depending on `flint:java`, `java-net` and `java-net-socket-address`), the `InetSocketAddress` probe is unchanged. On venus under v4.2, the package probe (the `null`-address rejection and its message) measures 48,124 O3 loadable bytes and 514 DMD templates (caps 52,937 B / 566, ceil 1.10x). The `flint.java.net.proxy` leaf probe (constructor rejection paths, `equals`, `hashCode`, `toString` and `type`, importing only the leaf) measures 48,972 O3 / 44,588 Oz loadable bytes and 279 DMD templates (caps 53,896 B / 307), with only the 16 inherited `flint.java.lang` records. As with S-157 and S-158, most of those bytes are `InetSocketAddress`'s case tables, which `Proxy`'s virtual `toString` and `equals` keep.

**S-159. Give `java.net.PasswordAuthentication` its own `framework/java-net-auth` package, because in `java-net-exceptions` it sorted first and became the batch's first root.** `passwordauthentication.d` sorts before `unknownhostexception.d`, so under v4.2 batch scope it took over as the first object, and the `flint.java.net.unknownhostexception` probe gained its module record (two violations). In its own package, which depends only on `flint:java`, the package probe (construct and read both fields) measures 488 O3 loadable bytes and 86 DMD templates on venus under v4.2 (caps 537 B / 95, ceil 1.10x). The leaf probe (copy, then clear the returned password) measures 1,072 O3 / 1,056 Oz loadable bytes and 61 DMD templates (caps 1,180 B / 68), with no violations. Rule of thumb, from the coordinator on 2026-11-27: when a new JDK leaf would shift the first object of an existing package onto another probe, give it a package of its own.
**S-161. Give `java.net.InterfaceAddress` its own `framework/java-net-interface-address` package above `java-net-address`, because it is constructed only package-internally by `NetworkInterface` and its virtual `toString` keeps the `java.time`/`java.util` family in every probe that calls it.** `InterfaceAddress` has no public or protected constructor in Java; `NetworkInterface` fills its address, broadcast and mask length. Keeping it in `java-net-address` would make the address batch's first-object placement depend on a type that only `NetworkInterface` will use, and would put its virtual `toString`, `equals` and `hashCode` into the `InetAddress` archive. In its own package (depending on `flint:engine-aot`, `flint:java` and `java-net-address`), the package probe (construct a v4 address and read its three fields) measures 9,360 O3 loadable bytes and 507 DMD templates on venus under v4.2 (caps 10,296 B / 558, ceil 1.10x). The `flint.java.net.interfaceaddress` leaf probe (default and v4 addresses, accessors, equality, hash and text) measures 16,968 O3 / 15,016 Oz loadable bytes and 269 DMD templates (caps 18,665 B / 296), with the eight inherited `java.sql`/`java.text.simpledateformat`/`java.time`/`java.util` records in each of O3 and Oz, no `flint.java.character` records, and no undeclared `java-net` module records. A compact case table in `flint:java` (S-157) would not change this leaf, which never compares host names.
**S-163. Split parsing-only `java.net.URL` and `MalformedURLException` into `framework/java-net-url` and `framework/java-net-url-exception`, because in one batch archive the exception probe linked `url.o` and `ipv6literal`.** `malformedurlexception.d` sorts first in a shared batch, so under v4.2 batch scope the `throw-catch` probe gained the `flint.java.net.url` and `flint.java.net.internal.ipv6literal` module records (and URL's `flint.java.character` tables) from `url.o`, four violations the closure gate cannot allow: neither module is a known inherited record. Putting the exception in its own package below URL leaves the exception probe with only the nine inherited `java.character`/`java.sql`/`java.time`/`java.util` records, and URL declares the exception leaf explicitly. On venus under v4.2, the `framework/java-net-url` package probe (parse a literal URL and read its components) measures 29,440 O3 loadable bytes and 635 DMD templates (caps 32,384 B / 699, ceil 1.10x); the `flint.java.net.url` leaf probe (absolute and relative parsing, component getters, `toExternalForm`, `sameFile`, equality and hash) measures 30,184 O3 / 28,060 Oz loadable bytes and 396 DMD templates (caps 33,103 B / 436), with the nine inherited records in each profile and no undeclared `java-net` records. The `framework/java-net-url-exception` package probe (construct and catch) measures 3,092 O3 loadable bytes and 436 DMD templates (caps 3,402 B / 480); the `flint.java.net.malformedurlexception` leaf probe measures 3,204 O3 / 2,020 Oz loadable bytes and 220 DMD templates (caps 3,525 B / 242), with the same nine inherited records. Both leaves keep `flint.java.lang` in their declared leaves so the inherited `java.lang` record stays declared rather than baseline debt.
**S-165. Give `java.net.HttpCookie` its own `framework/java-net-http-cookie` package, because it is a value type that parses with `List!HttpCookie` and reads the clock, and it must not join the `java-net` codec batch.** `HttpCookie.parse` returns the collection facade and `hasExpired` reads `engine-aot`'s clock, so the type needs `flint:java` and `flint:engine-aot`; putting it in the codec package would add its `List!HttpCookie` and clock instances to every codec probe. On venus under v4.2, the package probe (parse a `Set-Cookie` header, then domain-match and expiry) measures 50,368 O3 loadable bytes and 816 DMD templates (caps 55,405 B / 898, ceil 1.10x). The `flint.java.net.httpcookie` leaf probe (parse, attributes, `hasExpired`, equality and text) measures 50,536 O3 / 46,040 Oz loadable bytes and 540 DMD templates (caps 55,590 B / 594), with the eight inherited `java.character`/`java.sql`/`java.time`/`java.util` records in each profile; `flint.java.lang` and `flint.java.util` are declared leaves, and there are no undeclared `java-net` records.

**S-166. Give `java.net.ProtocolException` and `java.net.UnknownServiceException` their own `framework/java-net-protocol-exception` and `framework/java-net-unknown-service-exception` packages instead of adding them to `java-net-exceptions`.** The checked-exception batch holds `unknownhostexception.d` as its first executable root; a `protocolexception.d` that sorts before it would take that root and shift the `UnknownHostException` probe's retained record, the same way `passwordauthentication.d` did (S-146/S-159). Each leaf here has one type, depends only on `flint:java`, and keeps its own package so neither can change the other's first-object placement. On venus under v4.2, both package probes (construct and catch the checked `IOException` subclass with and without a detail message) measure 3,092 O3 loadable bytes and 436 DMD templates (caps 3,402 B / 480, ceil 1.10x). Both leaf `throw-catch` probes measure 3,204 O3 / 2,020 Oz loadable bytes and 220 DMD templates (caps 3,525 B / 242), retaining the same nine inherited `java.character`/`java.sql`/`java.text.simpledateformat`/`java.time`/`java.util` records in each profile and no undeclared `java-net` records. Both leaves declare `flint.java.io` and `flint.java.lang` so the inherited `java.lang` record stays declared rather than baseline debt.

**S-168. Give `java.net.SocketTimeoutException` its own `framework/java-net-socket-timeout-exception` package, because it extends the `flint.java.io.interruptedioexception` leaf and must not change the `java-net-exceptions` batch root.** Java's `SocketTimeoutException` extends `java.io.InterruptedIOException`, so its package depends on `flint:java-io` as well as `flint:java`; putting it among the plain `IOException` leaves would add that archive member to their first-object placement and shift the `UnknownHostException` probe (the `protocolexception.d` problem of S-166). A one-type package keeps the dependency one-way and the batch probes unchanged. On venus under v4.2, the package probe (construct and catch the checked `InterruptedIOException` subclass with and without a detail message) measures 3,652 O3 loadable bytes and 436 DMD templates (caps 4,018 B / 480, ceil 1.10x). The `flint.java.net.sockettimeoutexception` leaf `throw-catch` probe measures 3,764 O3 / 2,580 Oz loadable bytes and 220 DMD templates (caps 4,141 B / 242), retaining the same nine inherited `java.character`/`java.sql`/`java.text.simpledateformat`/`java.time`/`java.util` records in each profile and no undeclared `java-net` records. The leaf declares `flint.java.io`, `flint.java.io.interruptedioexception` and `flint.java.lang`; `bytesTransferred` stays a public field on the inherited leaf.
**S-171. Port `java.util.jar`'s `Manifest` and `Attributes` without importing the aggregate `flint.java.util` container module.** The first draft implemented `Attributes` over `HashMap!(Any, Any)` and `Manifest.getEntries()` as `Map!(string, Attributes)`. That linked the whole `flint.java.util` module: 722,055 O3 bytes and 8,009 DMD templates, including `std.conv` and the calendar/time family. `flint.java.lang` already imports `flint.java.util` for `Iterator`, so the eight legacy `java.character`/`java.sql`/`java.time`/`java.util` records remain; nothing else should. The leaf now stores its few attributes and entry names in insertion-ordered D arrays and exposes its own `Entries` facade, keeping Java's method names. On venus under v4.2, the package probe (write and read a manifest) measures 50,468 O3 loadable bytes and 763 DMD templates (caps 55,515 B / 840, ceil 1.10x, `new-module-rule`). The `flint.java.util.jar` leaf probe (construct main and entry sections and read one) measures 45,396 O3 / 41,836 Oz loadable bytes and 535 DMD templates (caps 49,936 B / 589), with the eight inherited records in each profile and no Phobos or `java-net` records; `flint.java.io`, `flint.java.lang` and `flint.java.util` are declared leaves. Enforced by the framework size and template budgets and the JDK link-closure gate.


**S-173. Give `java.net.SocketException` its own `framework/java-net-socket-exception` package, because it is the base of the remaining socket exceptions and must not become the `java-net-exceptions` batch root.** `socketexception.d` sorts before `unknownhostexception.d`, so joining that package would take its first executable root and shift the `UnknownHostException` probe, the same failure as `passwordauthentication.d` (S-159). The package depends only on `flint:java` (it extends `IOException`), so the subclasses (`BindException`, `ConnectException`, `NoRouteToHostException`, `PortUnreachableException`) can each sit in their own package above it. On venus under v4.2, the package probe (construct and catch the checked `IOException` subclass with a detail message, a null message and a cause) measures 3,092 O3 loadable bytes and 436 DMD templates (caps 3,402 B / 480, ceil 1.10x). The `flint.java.net.socketexception` leaf `throw-catch` probe measures 3,204 O3 / 2,020 Oz loadable bytes and 220 DMD templates (caps 3,525 B / 242), retaining the same nine inherited `java.character`/`java.sql`/`java.text.simpledateformat`/`java.time`/`java.util` records in each profile and no undeclared `java-net` records. The leaf declares `flint.java.io` and `flint.java.lang`.

**S-175. Keep each `SocketException` subclass in its own one-type package above `framework/java-net-socket-exception`.** `BindException`, `ConnectException`, `NoRouteToHostException` and `PortUnreachableException` all extend `SocketException`. If they joined the base package, their source files would sort before some existing probes' roots and shift the base package's first-object placement, the same failure as S-166 and S-173. Each package therefore depends only on `flint:java-net-socket-exception`, and its leaf declares `flint.java.io`, `flint.java.lang` and `flint.java.net.socketexception`. On venus under v4.2, `BindException`'s package probe (construct and catch the checked `SocketException` subclass with and without a detail message) measures 3,572 O3 loadable bytes and 436 DMD templates (caps 3,930 B / 480, ceil 1.10x); its `throw-catch` leaf probe measures 3,684 O3 / 2,500 Oz loadable bytes and 220 DMD templates (caps 4,053 B / 242), with the same nine inherited `java.character`/`java.sql`/`java.text.simpledateformat`/`java.time`/`java.util` records in each profile and no undeclared `java-net` records.

**S-176. `java.net.ConnectException` gets its own `framework/java-net-connect-exception` package above `java-net-socket-exception`, for the same first-object reason as S-175.** On venus under v4.2, the package probe (construct and catch the checked `SocketException` subclass with and without a detail message) measures 3,588 O3 loadable bytes and 436 DMD templates (caps 3,947 B / 480, ceil 1.10x). Its `throw-catch` leaf probe measures 3,700 O3 / 2,516 Oz loadable bytes and 220 DMD templates (caps 4,071 B / 242), with the same nine inherited records in each profile and no undeclared `java-net` records.

**S-177. `java.net.NoRouteToHostException` gets its own `framework/java-net-no-route-to-host-exception` package above `java-net-socket-exception`, for the same first-object reason as S-175.** On venus under v4.2, the package probe (construct and catch the checked `SocketException` subclass with and without a detail message) measures 3,588 O3 loadable bytes and 436 DMD templates (caps 3,947 B / 480, ceil 1.10x). Its `throw-catch` leaf probe measures 3,700 O3 / 2,516 Oz loadable bytes and 220 DMD templates (caps 4,071 B / 242), with the same nine inherited records in each profile and no undeclared `java-net` records.

**S-178. `java.net.PortUnreachableException` gets its own `framework/java-net-port-unreachable-exception` package above `java-net-socket-exception`, for the same first-object reason as S-175.** On venus under v4.2, the package probe (construct and catch the checked `SocketException` subclass with and without a detail message) measures 3,604 O3 loadable bytes and 436 DMD templates (caps 3,965 B / 480, ceil 1.10x). Its `throw-catch` leaf probe measures 3,716 O3 / 2,532 Oz loadable bytes and 220 DMD templates (caps 4,088 B / 242), with the same nine inherited records in each profile and no undeclared `java-net` records.

**S-179. Give `java.net.HttpRetryException` its own `framework/java-net-http-retry-exception` package, because it is the remaining standalone `java.net` checked exception and must not become the `java-net-exceptions` batch root.** Joining `java-net-exceptions` would put `httpretryexception.d` among the `IOException` leaves and shift the `UnknownHostException` probe's first-object placement, the same failure as S-166 and S-173. The package depends only on `flint:java`, since the class extends `IOException` directly. On venus under v4.2, the package probe (construct and catch the checked `IOException` subclass, reading its response code and reason) measures 3,412 O3 loadable bytes and 436 DMD templates (caps 3,754 B / 480, ceil 1.10x). The `flint.java.net.httpretryexception` leaf `throw-catch` probe measures 3,532 O3 / 2,356 Oz loadable bytes and 220 DMD templates (caps 3,886 B / 242), retaining the same nine inherited `java.character`/`java.sql`/`java.text.simpledateformat`/`java.time`/`java.util` records in each profile and no undeclared `java-net` records. The leaf declares `flint.java.io` and `flint.java.lang`.

## 3. Build time

| ID | Rule | Evidence | Enforced by |
|---|---|---|---|
| B-1 | **Erased engine, thin typed façade** (ADR-030 §2). Generic public APIs are one-line templates over a non-template engine driven by descriptors. A template instantiated by user code is compiled in the user's build, every build. | `std.format.format!(char,string)` was 26 ms of a 57 ms served compile; `String.format` now runs over one non-template `Formatter` (`ea038af1`). Java's own erasure for lists of a class: `ArrayList`, `List.of`/`copyOf`/`singletonList`, `emptyList` and `unmodifiableList` of a class share one non-template `ErasedList`/`ErasedArrayList` over `Object` and add only thin typed methods (engine-aot `sys/erasure` views an `Object` as its known class: no per-element cast). Lists of a D interface share the same erased storage (decision of 2026-11-26 20:45Z, `7138019c5`, from sol-jdk-core's `18db93dd6`); typed reads do a dynamic interface cast, and `ArrayList!E` is no longer an `AbstractList!E` (a deviation registered once on those rows). Struct and scalar elements keep the templates. petclinic-rest Oz (`--profile size`, stripped) 10,980,632 to 10,683,768 B (-296,864); `flint.java.util` symbols 954 KB to 752 KB (AbstractList 132 to 33 KB, AbstractCollection 131 to 50 KB, mergeSort 59 to 18 KB). Owner 1 also 549.8k to 532.9k instructions and 279 to 243 allocations: `foreach` over these lists no longer allocates an iterator. Sets of a class likewise (`HashSet`/`LinkedHashSet` over one `ErasedHashSet`, so every map's `entrySet` of `MapEntry` shares it): Oz a further 10,683,768 to 10,565,784 B; both slices together -414,848 B (-3.8%); owner 1 532.9k to 530.6k instructions, allocations unchanged. | Template budgets: corrected count ×1.25, then only down (`e765293f`, `e849cb88`) |
| B-2 | **Avoid heavyweight Phobos templates** in anything a user build compiles: `std.format`, `std.datetime`, `std.path.buildPath`, `std.conv.to` chains, ranges and algorithms. Prefer small hand-written helpers, with a test against the Phobos result. | `001075b0`: `buildPath` took flint:config's compile from 56 to 110 ms on non-Linux hosts. `9db7fcd5`: context off `std.datetime`. | Template budgets |
| B-3 | **Generated code instantiates nothing per type that it can look up.** Reuse the module's own descriptors, use literal canonical names, and skip annotation types the shared cache already describes. | `3a5bfc94` (no `descriptorOf!(T)`), `d29a3926` (erased containers), `e755d28a`, `1b3b2c63` (raw checked cache entries, models parsed once), `c26facb0`. The per-endpoint fast path is table-driven (S1 slice): the runtime binds from the row's `ArgumentSpec` and calls the existing `handleArray_<id>`, so no endpoint gets a `fast_<id>` of its own (petclinic-rest's 37 mappings, 385 KB of Oz headroom). petclinic-rest and gs-relational-data-access generated output byte-identical; gs-rest-service loses its one `fast_` function, 18/18 exact, `/greeting` still 1 allocation / 16 B (its size and instruction delta not re-measured yet). | Generated-module compile times in gs-builds phases |
| B-3a | **A compile-time describer memoizes every piece it repeats, keyed by exactly what the piece reads.** Write per-type and per-value pieces (a type reference, a quoted file path, an attribute tuple, a supertype list) as `enum` templates (`enum string typeRefOf(T) = typeRef!T();`), so the compiler evaluates each once per compile. Calling a CTFE function again evaluates it again. Never use a recursive template for membership tests: one level per member makes O(members²) instances per type. Evaluate a piece once, not again in a `static if` and the concatenation. | Flint AOT's describer on petclinic-rest (95 types, 697 methods, 725 KB of model JSON): extraction compile 672 → 348 ms unserved, best of 3 (local, -48%), output byte-identical; the certified extraction was 667 of the AOT step's 760 ms (6ab588f8). The remaining ~190 ms is one `function_` instance per method, linear in methods. | Old/new describer byte-identity on the three Phase 1 apps; gs-builds phase budgets after proof-harness's re-run |
| B-4 | **Cache by content and by the exact files a tool reads, never by directory mtimes.** Build tools write outputs into package directories. | 78 ms of every cached cold build was a stale platform description. DUB drops libraries into package roots, and flint:term's pre-build rewrites its object. `6ab588f8` watches the root recipe files by content or absence instead. The certified flint phase went from 78 to 21 ms, and the primary from 1.255 to 0.933 of Go (`7a3f7e53`). | `test_a_build_of_another_configuration_keeps_the_platform_description` and the platformcache unittest |
| B-5 | **Keep DUB and hashing off the hot paths.** Dev builds compile and link directly, replaying DUB's recorded commands, with staleness tests. Dev receipts hash outputs lazily. The platform description and digests live in `FLINT_CACHE`. | `bf7b7b97`, `dc711c95`, `2d8a16dc`, `804c2b6c`, `87c13a93`. Cached cold 3.07 → 1.255 of Go; shared edit 0.398. | gs-builds phase budgets |
| B-5a | **Compile a dev build per module, and recompile only what an edit can reach.** One whole-program compile makes every edit pay for the whole project. Each root module gets its own served job and object, in parallel. An edit recompiles the changed modules and their importers, with any text change counting, because importers can see bodies through CTFE, templates and auto functions. Do not use `dmd -deps` to find imports: it made one module's compile 1,026 ms instead of 118. Scan the import declarations instead. | petclinic-rest, local: an edit compiled all 28 modules in one job (served 445 ms) while the edited module alone takes 110 ms. After per-module compilation the edit compile is 131-163 ms (was 490-595) and the cold compile 240-262 ms (was 530-715). The link is unchanged at ~40 ms, and no Phase 1 app needs -allinst. Certified numbers wait for proof-harness. | `test_a_dev_edit_recompiles_only_the_modules_it_can_affect`; `tools/incremental_equivalence.py` (petclinic-rest, 12 seeded steps; gs-relational-data-access, 8) |
| B-6 | **Dev builds link shared; static links use 8 threads.** | Owner decision on dev shared linking. `df11097e`: static link 174 → 136 ms; `943466fa` makes 8 the default. | gs-builds |
| B-7 | **A served compile produces exactly what an in-process compile produces,** or fails loudly. | Missing instances fail as FLINT-E0701 (prelude, `42ebbef9`). `027721b6`: a failed served link used to exit 0. | Parity tests in the fork |
| B-8 | **Key a code generator on module interfaces and on the content of what it extracted, never on source text alone.** Flint AOT's extraction key hashes a module's interface: its types and the declarations they reference, not function bodies, unittests or unreferenced top-level constants. The output key hashes the extracted models by content, so a re-extraction that reproduces them writes nothing (early cutoff). Platform modules' models and the generated output live in `FLINT_CACHE`, so a clean `.flint` restores them instead of re-extracting. | Local, optimized tool, gs-rest-service. `1397cf87`: the benchmark edit (an appended enum) 81 → 3 ms, nothing extracted or written. `a9def752`: cold AOT step 604 → 143 ms, because Boot's 22 auto-configurations are no longer re-extracted per project. `7907be4e`: cached cold 665 → 13 ms, byte-identical output. | `aot_test`: incremental equals full, shared/cold/corrupt cache tests; gs-builds phase budgets |
| B-9 | **A tool's helper compile runs with the same compile-server context as the build's own compile, and a trace proves it was served.** Flint AOT's extraction compile gets the platform prelude under exactly the project compile's condition (active platform, server-enabled profile, non-test dev build); the platform step runs first. Check with a private `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` short enough for a Unix socket path (`sun_path` is 108 bytes; a longer one silently compiles in-process) and `FLINT_DMD_SERVER_TRACE`: `compiled`, not `refused`, on the second build. | petclinic-rest on the cbcd05fd fork toolchain (venus, 2026-11-24): an edit-shaped extraction (15 project modules, platform models cached) is one served compile, `compiled` in the trace, 32 and 30 ms through the native extractor, against 402 ms through the describer unserved on the older shared fork. Edit builds 139–143 ms with every compile served (2 per build, 0 refused). The shared fork built before 593f1b1a refused every prelude request; the fork's age, not the wiring, was the cause. Generated output byte-identical between the served native build and a stock describer build. A served request must carry every `-version=` its prelude defines: B2's gs-rest cold builds had exactly one refused request each, the extraction, because natives add `FlintNative*` versions to the prelude and the extraction passed none (AOT 98 ms, extraction 80 ms in-process); passing the prelude's versions made it served (AOT 23-25 ms, extraction 6-8 ms, local harness cold rules), generated output unchanged on the three apps. | Trace check (second build, `compiled`); native/describer parity on the three apps and tests/aot |
| B-10 | **Look at the link's arguments before the linker.** DUB passes every package's native linker flags once per dependency path to it and never drops a repeat, so a deep platform names the same archives thousands of times. When flint records a static dev build's link it merges the repeated archives and `-l` flags that follow the platform's group into one `--start-group ... --end-group` of each input once, in first-seen order. A group resolves whatever any number of repeats did, with ld.bfd too; mold ignores groups. | petclinic-rest static dev edit (venus, local, the cbcd05fd fork with mold and 8 link threads, 2026-11-24): the recorded link had 7,792 arguments, with libflint_ffi_base.a 1,431 times, libyaml.a 1,186 times and -lpthread/-ldl/-lm 1,431 times each: 3,463 archive mentions for 67 archives. The link falls from 318 to 128 ms on the same objects (median of 5), 138–148 ms inside `flint build`. The edit build falls from 745–766 to 559–596 ms. The executable is byte-identical to DUB's (cmp). Certified at B (b9357220) before the change: static edit 755 ms = compile 395 + link 313, 2.53x Go. Not yet certified after it. | `collapseRepeatedInputs` unittest (merge, unchanged with no repeats, a flag or object between repeats leaves the link as it was); cmp of the replayed binary against DUB's own |
| B-11 | **An incremental build needs a one-module-per-compile cold build under it.** A static dev build runs DUB with `--build-mode=singleFile --parallel` and records every compile; a replay reruns only the modules an edit can affect, with B-5a's rules (changed text and transitive importers; a stamp over compiler, flags, string imports and linked archives). A whole compile places each template instance in one object, so a partial recompile on top of it could drop an instance that another object relied on; with one compile per module from the first build on, each object is complete for its module. Two traps found on the way. The Flint DUB's unbuffered stderr trace landed inside 4 KiB blocks of `-v` stdout: 2 of 10 cold builds recorded no replay, and DUB's compile phase was charged to plan. flint now moves spliced trace lines back onto their own lines. And with 28 compiles, the replay's watched source trees were walked 28 times each, 18 ms per edit, now walked once. | petclinic-rest static dev edit (venus, local, the cbcd05fd fork with mold, 8 link threads, with B-10, 2026-11-24): edit build 298–307 ms (flint 37–41, aot 4, compile 112–120 for 2 of 28 modules, link 137–144), down from 745–766; cold static build 860–947 ms, down from about 990. Go's incremental build is 298 ms at B, so about 1.02x locally, against 2.53x certified at B. Not yet certified. 12–16 mold threads link in 127 ms against 138 at 8 (not A/B'd). | tests/test_flint_platform_cache.py: a static edit recompiles only the modules it can affect (compile-time value check), and a replay equals a fresh one-module-per-compile DUB build byte for byte; `separateTrace` unittest; `incremental_equivalence.py --static` (symbols against DUB's whole compile, bytes against a fresh one-module-per-compile build, in-process compiles) |
| B-12 | **One ineligible package can serialize a whole build: check what the scheduler rejects, not just what it runs.** The Flint DUB's parallel pipeline rejected any graph with a pre-build command or a C source. flint:term (a pre-build `sh native/build.sh` that builds its terminal object) and flint:os (`interrupt_latch.c`) sit under flint:boot-core, so every Boot application's cold and scratch builds compiled its 50-odd packages one after another. The pipeline now runs pre-build commands exactly when the sequential build would (out-of-date targets only, same function, directory and environment), in dependency order before the first compile job, and compiles C sources through ImportC in their target's single job. | Scratch builds (`flint build --force --no-platform-cache --no-debug`), venus, local, the cbcd05fd fork with mold, 2026-11-24, current DUB against patched, 2 trials each: petclinic-rest 5.49–5.75 s to 1.38–1.41 s (DUB compile 4,821 to 585 ms: 455 modules in 57 parallel jobs), gs-rest-service 3.13–3.28 to 0.72–0.93 s, gs-relational-data-access 2.49–2.52 to 0.66–0.83 s. Petclinic acceptance 109/109 on the pipeline-built scratch binary. These runs hit the shared AOT cache; certified at B: petclinic scratch 5,744 ms, 1.417x Go. Lands in the shared toolchain with the next swap (.tools-v4). | tests/test_flint_toolchain.py DubPipelineTests: a pre-build package and a C-source package take the pipeline; the pre-build runs once, before a dependent's compile that string-imports its output; the C source compiles in its target's one job. It fails on the cbcd05fd fork |
| B-13 | **The platform's archive layout follows from the DUB path that built it.** The recursive path archives with `dmd -lib`, one object per function: petclinic's static link read 666 MB and parsed 56,599 members to link 16,812. The parallel pipeline (B-12) archives one object per module, so mold reads far less and the static edit's link drops from about 135 to 80 ms. It also exposed a latent flint bug: the static link never named the platform modules' object sources (flint:term's `terminal-linux.o`), which DUB hands to the final link rather than to the archive. Per-function members happened not to pull the module that calls the C shim; whole-module members do. The shared link always named them; the static link now does too (`platformObjects`). | petclinic-rest static dev edit, venus, local, cbcd05fd fork with mold and 8 threads, platform primed by the B-12 DUB, with B-10/B-11, 2026-11-24: 239–252 ms (link 78–87), against 297–304 ms on the `dmd -lib` platform and 298 ms for Go's incremental build, about 0.82x locally. Cold static 805–898 ms. Priming the platform takes 2.4 s instead of 6.6. The dev static executable grows from about 21.9 MB to 28.5 MB, because whole modules are linked; the Oz gate builds with LDC and is unaffected. Petclinic acceptance 109/109 on the static binary. Not certified; it reaches the harness with the shared .tools-v4. | `platformObjects` unittest; incremental_equivalence.py --static on the new layout (petclinic 6 steps, gs-rest-service 5 with acceptance) |
| B-14 | **A one-module app's edit build is that module's compile: profile it with `-ftime-trace` before touching the build tool.** gs-relational-data-access recompiles its own module and its AOT module; the app module took 87 ms served, of which about 34 ms instantiated Phobos `std.format.format!(char, const(long), string, string)` for `Customer.toString`, an instance unique to the app that the compile server's prelude cannot share. The guide's Java calls `String.format`; flint:java's `String.format` runs one non-template `Formatter` compiled into the platform (ea038af1, as gs-rest-service already used). | gs-relational-data-access static dev edit, venus, local, v4.2 with mold and 8 threads, 2026-11-24, 5 trials: 121–127 ms (compile 46–47, link 51–59, flint 19–21), down from 164–174 ms (compile 82–90). The app module's served compile: 87 to 53 ms. Certified at B2 before the change: 161 ms, 1.053x Go's 153 ms. Acceptance 1/1, wire exact, on dev and release.
