Not certified yet. | -ftime-trace of the module; gs-relational acceptance 1/1 wire exact |
| B-15 | **Provenance a receipt needs can run while the build runs.** A receipt's source identity (`git rev-parse HEAD`, then `git status --porcelain`) ran serially after the link: about 8 ms of a 120 ms edit build. flint now starts both commands when the build starts (`startSourceIdentity`, through `std.process`) and collects them when the receipt is written; the record describes the sources as the build found them, and falls back to the synchronous commands if git could not start. | gs-relational-data-access static dev edit, venus, local, v4.2 with mold, 2026-11-24, 5 trials, with B-14: 119–124 ms (flint 15–16 ms, down from 19–23), from 121–127. The receipt records the same commit and dirty state. | test_flint_cli and test_flint_platform_cache (receipt source fields); flint unittests |
| B-90 | **A framework layer that runs for every request earns a fast path by stating its effect once, at startup, from its own code.** The per-endpoint fast path (fast-path.md) grew from gs-rest-service's shape to petclinic-rest's: S1 path variables, S2 `ResponseEntity`, S3 `@CrossOrigin` `Vary`, S4 the security filter chain, each proved against the configured strategies at startup and declining before any observable action. All of it is table-driven: no per-endpoint generated code. | petclinic-rest `GET /petclinic/api/owners/1` (owner 1), fast path off against on at the same tree (LDC release, callgrind over real HTTP, one reactor, 300 measured requests): **494.5k → 400.8k instructions per request (−18.9%)**, GC allocations 164 → 128. Every request accepted (one `serveEntity`, no `ServletEngine.capture`, no `FilterChainProxy.doFilter`, no escapes). 109/109 wire exact both ways; gs-rest-service 18/18. Stripped release +55,200 B (+0.48%). | petclinic-rest A/B (`FLINT_AOT_FAST_PATH=0`) in the next named baseline; accept/decline counts per endpoint |
| B-16 | **Reuse concrete generic types and keep record operations non-templated.** Alias the attribute and nullable types once. Construct record text in one sized output buffer. Keep the validation import inside the constructor and force its inlining only on LDC. Reuse the same concrete map type for mutable copies and frozen snapshots. | `ai-model`, venus, pinned DMD and LDC 1.43.0. `TextPart` dropped from 1,229 to 1,184 DMD templates. `ReasoningPart` initially took the count to 1,241. | B-92 | **Keep bit sets in a packed non-template word vector.** Track the logical words separately from the allocated capacity and scan only those words with bit intrinsics. The canonical package probe measures 13,500 O3 and 10,564 Oz loadable bytes with 278 DMD templates. The closure probe uses its app-shaped control and measures 13,532 O3 and 10,596 Oz bytes. Matched venus probes `64f0653bc`, `4fbd8dd21` and `7cc0fcef9` measure 13,500, 15,100 and 14,004 O3 bytes and 10,564, 11,884 and 10,564 Oz bytes. DMD counts 278, 303 and 303 templates. Both compilers allocate zero GC bytes in the warmed range loop. The `andNot` probe measures 14,884 O3 and 11,428 Oz bytes with the trailing-word scan inlined. Sharing the scan across point, range and mask operations brings the result to 14,788 O3 and 11,364 Oz bytes, with 305 DMD templates in both candidates. The warmed mask loop allocates zero GC bytes on both compilers. Adding `intersects` with a shared cold null throw first measures 15,012 O3 and 11,572 Oz bytes. Sharing cold growth and the backwards search used by both previous-bit methods yields 14,772 O3 and 11,700 Oz bytes. DMD records 305 templates throughout and warmed intersection calls allocate zero GC bytes under both compilers. The first in-place `and` build adds a shared mask loop and measures 15,036 O3 and 11,964 Oz bytes with 305 templates. Sharing the forward search, expansion, and cold bounds throw and applying range masks in one loop brings it to 14,804 O3 and 11,684 Oz bytes and 292 templates. Warmed in-place calls allocate zero GC bytes. Applying masks to the range edges once leaves a full-word operation in the interior. The matched probe moves from 14,804 O3 and 11,684 Oz to 14,708 O3 and 11,908 Oz bytes at 292 DMD templates. The existing range oracle and warmed allocation test pass. Counting from the last live word retains the per-word popcount and reduces O3/Oz to 14,460/11,628 bytes. Sharing the first word read between the clipped and in-range backward search paths gives 14,412/11,580 bytes, from a matched 14,708/11,908 base. DMD remains at 292 templates. The existing scalar search, cardinality oracle and allocation tests pass on DMD and LDC. Once expansion itself is out of line, the doubling calculation can live in that single helper. The matched standalone probe moves from 14,412/11,580 to 14,300/11,492 O3/Oz bytes at 292 DMD templates. Point set, clear and flip share validation and the word update. A clear still exits before capacity expansion. The matched probe changes from 14,300/11,492 to 14,228/11,428 O3/Oz bytes at 292 templates. Both compilers pass the existing point-operation and allocation tests. The physical word bound also guards point reads because unused capacity holds zero words. Matched probes move from 14,228/11,428 to 14,188/11,380 O3/Oz bytes at 292 DMD templates. Both compilers check reads from retained capacity after point clears, flips and a complete clear. Check range bounds once by slicing the affected words before applying the two edge masks. Adding an owned slice copy for `toLongArray` with this change moves the matched standalone probe from 14,188/11,380 to 14,300/11,516 O3/Oz bytes and from 292 to 293 DMD templates. In the diagnostic composition with equality and JavaObject, the range slice changes 14,860/12,060 to 14,796/12,012 at 295 templates, and both linked profiles pass the leaf cap. Point and range writes call the expansion helper only when they reach past the live words. The matched probe falls from 14,300/11,516 to 14,284/11,500 O3/Oz bytes at 293 DMD templates. The existing point, range and allocation tests cover the distinction between live words and spare capacity. Forward and backward searches share the word loop. The direction determines the initial mask, step, stopping word and bit intrinsic. The matched probe falls from 14,284/11,500 to 14,188/11,404 O3/Oz bytes at 293 DMD templates. Both compilers pass the scalar search model and the warmed allocation test. An out-of-range forward set-bit search now shares the negative-word exit with the backward search. The matched probe falls from 14,188/11,404 to 14,172/11,388 O3/Oz bytes at 293 templates. The two array exports share one owned word copy. Byte export returns a little-endian slice of that copy and trims whole trailing zero bytes. Its matched package probe measures 14,332/11,540 O3/Oz bytes with 293 DMD templates. The compound probe with equality measures 14,844/12,052 O3/Oz and 295 templates. The exact compound leaf is 14,876/12,084 under the owner-approved api-growth cap. Point and range mutations share the inclusive word update after their public bounds checks. Point updates pass the same index twice, so the helper avoids adding one to the largest Java index. The matched package probe changes from 14,332/11,540 to 14,196/11,372 O3/Oz bytes with 293 DMD templates. Both compilers exercise point and range operations, boundary checks, and warmed allocation. The four searches share their index validation while the word scan stays allocation-free. The matched probe changes from 14,196/11,372 to 14,132/11,324 O3/Oz bytes at 293 DMD templates. Both compilers check the directional index messages and the scalar search model. Whole-set clear and intersection with a shorter mask share the trailing-word clear. The matched package probe changes from 14,132/11,324 to 14,108/11,316 O3/Oz bytes at 293 DMD templates. Both compilers exercise capacity, masked writes, and warmed allocation. Reads and mutations compute unsigned word offsets after their signed Java bounds checks. The matched probe changes from 14,108/11,316 to 14,076/11,268 O3/Oz bytes at 293 DMD templates. The signed-word factory trims zero words before allocating and shares the bounded slice copy with both exports. Keeping its runtime call in the existing probe gives 14,348/11,548 O3/Oz bytes and 295 templates. The compound probe with equality measures 14,844/12,044 O3/Oz package bytes and 297 templates, with an exact leaf of 14,876/12,076. The array exports share the live-word slice calculation before copying. Matched package O3/Oz compares 14,348/11,548 with 14,348/11,540 at 295 DMD templates. A point or range clear or flip can shorten the live word span only when it reaches the last live word. The mutation helper skips the trailing scan for interior writes. Matched package O3/Oz compares 14,348/11,540 with 14,348/11,556 at 295 templates. Both compilers pass point, range, capacity and warmed allocation tests. Recalculation scans with a local remaining count and updates the live count once after finding the last nonzero word. Matched package O3/Oz remain 14,348/11,556 bytes at 295 DMD templates. The directional bit search uses one masked load site and resets the mask after the first word. Matched package O3/Oz fall from 14,348/11,556 to 14,332/11,508 bytes at 295 DMD templates. Keep point and range validation out of line so their mutation entry points share validation code. The matched probe measures 14,292/11,468 O3/Oz bytes from 14,332/11,508 at 295 DMD templates. Self-difference clears the live words directly and skips the backward trim of the cleared tail. The matched probe grows from 14,292/11,468 to 14,308/11,484 O3/Oz bytes at 295 DMD templates. If two masks share no live words, the operation can return after any required clear without rereading the tail. Matched package sizes change from 14,308/11,484 to 14,348/11,524 O3/Oz bytes at 295 DMD templates. Equality and hashing mix in JavaObject from its selective module and compare only the live words. The package probe grows from 14,348 to 14,844 O3 bytes and from 295 to 297 DMD templates. The exact leaf grows from a matched 14,380/11,556 to 14,876/12,052 O3/Oz bytes, which is the owner-approved api-growth cap. The in-place `or` and `xor` share the existing mask loop; the matched leaf moves from 14,876/12,052 to 15,428/12,604 O3/Oz bytes at 297 DMD templates, and the package probe from 14,850 to 15,396 O3 bytes, within the owner-approved api-growth caps. Constructing from little-endian bytes reuses the owned word vector and the signed-word factory for the empty case. It leaves both linked profiles unchanged (package 15,396 O3 / 12,572 Oz bytes, exact leaf 15,428/12,604 O3/Oz) and the package compile counts 300 DMD templates against the approved 306; the bench fixture does not call the new entry point, so the linker drops its code. Range copies clamp the end to the live bits and take the words across a source-word boundary; `clone` copies the word array into independent storage, and the text form fills one sized character buffer. Matched O3 package and exact-leaf probes move from 15,444/15,476 to 17,660/17,692 bytes, with DMD templates from 306 to 308, within the owner-approved api-growth caps. | `java-collections` package and BitSet leaf budgets, O3/Oz JDK link-closure audit and linked allocation test. |
| B-132 | **Store an enum set as a word vector over declaration ordinals, choosing the representation at compile time.** A type's constants form a CTFE universe; sets of at most 64 constants keep one inline word and larger sets keep an array, so point operations are bit intrinsics and allocate nothing, while bulk operations between enum sets run word by word. The typed element parameter replaces Java's `Class<E>` and makes a foreign constant or a null element a compile error. | Venus, initial independent `EnumSet` leaf (this change). The `api` link-closure probe over the 330,117-byte control measures 56,876 O3 and 53,636 Oz incremental loadable bytes with 961 joint DMD template instances; the leaf and package caps are 62,564 bytes and 1,058 templates. The probe retains only the known legacy `flint.java.util` aggregate module records, seeded under decision 523bd5bd. The original `EnumSetOracle` transcript matches Temurin 21.0.13+9, and warmed point operations allocate zero GC bytes on DMD and LDC. | `java-collections` package and EnumSet leaf budgets, the O3/Oz JDK link-closure audit, the oracle test and the linked allocation test. |

table through a dependency-free leaf. The matched venus probes at

The Java probe measured 129,496 O3 bytes, 122,144 Oz bytes and 4,440 templates
in both builds. The isolated digit-leaf probe adds 704 O3 and 312 Oz loadable
bytes. Both profiles pass the JDK link-closure audit.

Return an existing scalar or the empty singleton for those key shapes, and copy retained array and text elements when building compound keys. | Matched Venus Context module gates on `b90f7693e` and `6181d32b8` measured 636,064 to 636,176 incremental linked bytes and 5,296 to 6,433 DMD template instances, within the original caps. | Context DMD and LDC tests, including the warm zero-GC checks and the linked AOT array key fixture. |
| B-65 | **Bound default stream skipping and advance byte-array streams directly.** The base method allocates one scratch array of at most 2 KiB and calls the ranged reader. Byte-array skip advances its position without copying, and ranged reads preserve aliased data. | Matched stock Java module probes on venus add 129,496 loadable bytes both before and after. DMD changes from 4,440 to 4,443 template instances. | Module budgets and direct Java 21 stream witnesses for dispatch, bounds and aliasing. |

| B-95 | **Format InvalidPathException when it is constructed.** The exception keeps the input, reason and index and serves the formatted detail through the inherited `@nogc` accessor. D-JDK-3 describes subclass message dispatch. R26 treats null strings as empty. | Venus pinned probes. After R26, `06bcabaec` added 8,692 O3 and 5,972 Oz loadable bytes with 459 templates. The prior string-null exception checks measured 9,356 O3 and 6,652 Oz loadable bytes with 461 templates. Initial leaf and package caps are 9,562 bytes and 505 templates. | java-nio-file definition of done, link closure and Temurin constructor cases. |

The Java form encoder converts its own fresh output buffer to an immutable
slice at the strongly pure return boundary. Over 2,000 warmed encodes of
`café plus +`, UTF-8 allocations fell from 128,000 to 64,000 bytes and
ISO-8859-1 allocations fell from 64,000 to 32,000 bytes. The ASCII input
that needs no escaping still allocates zero bytes. Charset value
comparisons also removed the decoder's decimal table from the stock O3
and Oz encoder probes. Both codec probes still report inherited Java
module records in the stock closure check.

With the shared BMP decimal digit leaf, the stock LDC single-leaf probes
add 15,244 O3 and 13,348 Oz loadable bytes for URLEncoder, and 17,588 O3
and 16,060 Oz for URLDecoder. Isolated DMD library and probe builds use
448 and 465 template instances respectively. The combined probe adds
20,676 O3 and 18,160 Oz bytes and uses 472 template instances before the
R26 null-slice changes. The stock closure audit accepts the 36 exact
legacy module records under decision 523bd5bd. The strict release check
still reports those records until the aggregate is split.

The fixed UTF-8 overloads add 7,676 O3/6,212 Oz bytes for the encoder
and 11,308 O3/9,428 Oz for the decoder. Named charset support adds the
charset alias lookup, checked exception translation and non-UTF-8
paths. Both codecs share the named lookup adapter. Malformed escapes
use a separate error formatter.

R26 treats null string slices as empty. Absent Charset values still
report NullPointerException through a shared cold helper. The resulting
named-codec probes add 14,828 O3 and 12,916 Oz bytes for URLEncoder
and 17,084 O3 and 15,580 Oz bytes for URLDecoder. The combined probe
adds 19,972 O3 and 18,876 Oz bytes and uses 467 templates. The first
package caps are 21,970 bytes and 514 templates. Isolated encoder and decoder
probes use 445 and 462 templates respectively.
| B-104 | **Import the JavaObject mixin from its selective module.** The compatibility export from flint.java.lang uses the same mixin body. | On venus the matched BitSet consumer drops from 218 to 29 DMD templates, and its package measurement drops from 483 to 294. Both imports link 15,220 O3 and 12,420 Oz bytes. The standalone JavaObject probe measures 1,061 O3 and 1,053 Oz bytes with 38 DMD templates. Generated AOT is byte-identical in all three benchmark apps. | JavaObject leaf budget and exact Java link closure. |
| B-97 | **Link only the target’s lexical Path provider.** The default factory links the Unix parser on POSIX and the Windows parser on Windows. Name operations use slices of the normalized spelling. The R7 facade passes strings in a stack array, and R26 treats null strings and arrays as empty. | Venus pinned probes after the string R26 clarification at `5a3c200a4` and `9e6426cb7`. Standalone Path adds 10,852 O3 and 9,316 Oz loadable bytes with 481 templates. The combined Path and exception fixture adds 13,436 O3 and 10,652 Oz bytes with 483 templates. The earlier probes with string-null exception paths measured 13,828 O3 bytes standalone and 14,108 combined. | Standalone and combined receipts, the two target objects, lexical contract tests and linker closure. |

## 4. Measuring honestly

| ID | Rule | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| M-1 | **Give Flint exactly the state Go gets.** Every cold trial starts from a fresh, platform-only cache, with no project-keyed reuse; the harness fails a trial that reuses project state. | `bf57dc9f`. The 2.52 primary at `2018e5e5` had reused the app's AOT output and was optimistic. |
| M-2 | **Match the comparator's flags:** `--no-debug`, against Go's `-s -w`. Publish the other variant as non-gating context. | Owner decision `4b27d207`. |
| M-3 | **Trace before fixing, and mirror the harness exactly when reproducing.** Priming order and the cache's history change results. | The 377 ms cold `flint` phase was a `.so` relink; the 78 ms was a stale record that only appeared after priming several configurations. |
| M-4 | **Measure in claimed windows with everything else paused,** pin base and candidate to the same conditions, and label combined changes honestly. | `docs/spring/phase1/coordination.md`, "Measurement". |
| M-5 | **A fully static Flint binary is a musl binary, and musl brings its own allocator.** Static linking under ADR-023 means the linux-x86_64-musl target (`flint package --target linux-x86_64-musl`, `--profile size` for Oz). Its malloc is mallocng, which is slower than glibc's ptmalloc on allocation-heavy paths and lays memory out differently. Every native library allocates through it: openssl, llhttp, nghttp2, libpq. So does druntime for memory outside the GC heap. A glibc-dynamic against musl-static A/B measures the libc and its allocator as well as the linking. Record that with the result, and do not attribute a throughput or p99 change to static linking alone. Go's comparator does not use libc malloc, so it has no such term. | Owner decision `6ecd8933` (release-static-ab). Local: gs-rest-service musl O3 is a 5,392,992-byte static-pie and passes 18/18 acceptance; certified numbers wait for proof-harness. |

## 5. Correctness traps found while optimising

Speed work introduced these, or exposed them. Check every optimisation against them.

- **Java formatter diagnostics use UTF-16 offsets (R14).** DateTimeFormatter parses UTF-8 bytes and converts the consumed prefix only when throwing. On venus, the formatter parse probe at base `d8f4e413` changed from 363,685 to 364,669 loadable bytes at O3 and from 359,733 to 360,485 at Oz. The probe retained the same JDK modules and banned-symbol set. DMD and LDC tests check the returned index and diagnostic text for invalid fields and trailing input after a BMP and supplementary character.
- **Shared or recycled objects across reactor threads.** Immutability, or a loud failure on misuse, plus a concurrent test (R-4, R-6).
- **Cancelled cache waiters retire their reactor wake handle.** The waiting task receives `IOException` after unregistering the handle. The loader remains governed by its own caller's task. The production-linked fixture in `framework/context/fixtures/cache-cancellation` checks shutdown on DMD and LDC. The context probe retained 646,236 loadable bytes and used 8,106 template instances after this change.
- **No ThreadSanitizer lane yet: druntime's collector blocks it.** LDC's `-fsanitize=thread` builds the framework tests, but the run never finishes. After 10 minutes on 32 CPUs the binary was still spinning in the stop-the-world collector, with no report (web-http, `351683be`). Until the collector cooperates with TSan (D3's concern), use concurrent tests whose failure is visible, checked against a planted fault: `concurrent_dispatch_tests` fails when a per-call field is planted on the shared invocable.
- **Thread-local storage in shared libraries.** The AArch64 backend used local-exec TLS under `-fPIC`, so a library's variables silently aliased the executable's. Initial-exec through the GOT is used now, with tests that cover `dlopen`.
- **Signal dispositions inherited by worker processes.** An ignored `SIGCHLD` turned linker failures into success (`027721b6`).
- **Caches and replays that go stale silently.** Test that adding a file, removing one, changing a string import, or restoring an mtime invalidates them (`87c13a93`, `c4a5a8b2`).
- **`typeid` of an interface reference names the interface,** not the object's class. Exact-class checks use `typeid(cast(Object) x)`. The fast path's startup proof silently declined every route until this was fixed, which only the allocation counter showed (fast-path.md §8.1).
- **A D interface default implemented by a mixin can bind a sibling method in the same mixin.** `Pageable.Defaults.isUnpaged()` called the mixed-in `isPaged()` and missed `Unpaged.isPaged()` on both DMD and LDC. Call through the `Pageable` reference to preserve virtual dispatch. The `toLimit` test exercises paged, unpaged and sorted unpaged requests. The unpaged result uses R-44's stateless instance and its factory compiles as `nothrow @nogc`.
- **A nested function's address stored in a field of a non-`scope` local struct makes D allocate a GC closure** of the whole frame, stack buffers included: 2 allocations and about 4 KB per request in the fast-path prototype. Declare the struct `scope`, and check with `-vgc` (fast-path.md §8.2).
- **Check caller qualifiers when adding a stream instance monitor.** In a local `ByteArrayOutputStream` proof at `69af0ca5b` against `9ab4007de`, a recording mutex on the stream observed one balanced lock and unlock per storage operation, including release when a ranged write threw. The test failed on the base and passed on DMD and LDC, including optimized builds. With `synchronized (this)`, both compilers retained `const`, `pure` and `@nogc` where the accessors already used them, and rejected existing-style `nothrow` callers of `size()`, `toByteArray()` and `reset()`. Check callers and app closure before changing those signatures.
- **Keep the inherited whole-array read when a byte-array stream owns the ranged copy.** Pinned Java dispatches a whole-array read through the virtual ranged overload. On the queued Flint input base `8ab53f30182f`, a ranged read instead reached a subclass's whole-array override. The private proof `83dde90da` put the existing overlap-safe copy directly under the ranged method's instance monitor and inherited the whole-array entry point. The original delegation test failed on the base. DMD and LDC passed both call directions, monitor release on an invalid range, and Java's empty-array EOF order, including optimized builds. Both compilers retained `pure @nogc` for `skip` and `available` and rejected their previous `nothrow` caller signatures.
- **Pay-for-use registration gaps.** Every typed path that can need the optional code must link it. The remaining gap is a recorded deviation with a loud message (D-JACKSON-10).
- **Build Period overflow diagnostics only after identifying the rejected numeric component.** The original Flint tests compare the cause's token and first overflowing digit with Java 21 and check zero GC bytes on a successful parse. A shared decimal loop serves Period rendering and the diagnostic. On venus with LDC 1.43.0, the unchanged Period link probe at `dd109d0e` measured 12,844 O3 and 10,612 Oz incremental loadable bytes. With the diagnostic it measured 13,548 and 11,324 bytes, with the same retained JDK modules. DMD counts were 462 and 463 within the unchanged 14,991-byte and 1,000-template caps.
- **Build YearMonth syntax diagnostics only after a parse failure.** The exception keeps the whole input. The message distinguishes malformed input from trailing text and previews up to 64 UTF-16 units. On venus with LDC 1.43.0, the YearMonth text probe at `7dc2f084` measured 13,008 O3 and 9,768 Oz incremental loadable bytes. Building the diagnostic and keeping year rendering in a non-inlined digit loop measured 14,128 and 11,432 bytes, with the same retained JDK modules. DMD template counts were 442 and 445 against caps of 14,309 bytes and 487 templates. The YearMonth unit tests on both compilers verify zero GC bytes on successful parsing, a single owned result on rendering, and Java 21 syntax diagnostics.
- **Share message construction between YearMonth syntax and resolution errors.** Format the parsed fields only when range validation throws and preserve the field error as a nested cause. Reuse the digit writer and checked slice copy for the diagnostics. On venus with LDC 1.43.0, the YearMonth text probe at `ed4ca2f7` measured 14,128 O3 and 11,432 Oz incremental loadable bytes. Sharing the exception constructor and fragment writes reduced the first resolution diagnostic build from 14,864 and 12,144 bytes to 14,296 and 11,616 bytes. DMD template counts were 445 before and 446 after, within the unchanged 14,309-byte and 487-template caps. Both compilers verify the Java 21 error cause chain and successful parse allocations.
- **Keep ZoneOffsetTransition geometry independent of the other date values.** Instantiate the nullable offset adapter at its call site and assemble the diagnostic from bounded slices. The first build pulled Year through a shared nullable template and MonthDay through a shared array concatenation template. The independent probes on venus with LDC 1.43.0 retain neither module after those changes. Geometry adds 10,948 O3 and 8,300 Oz incremental loadable bytes with 509 DMD template instances. Display adds 15,612 and 10,852 bytes with 483 instances. Both compilers verify that the numeric accessors, duration, offset validity and comparison allocate zero GC bytes.
- **Link the concrete transition only when a recurring rule is applied.** The caller-instantiated rule method uses the shared calendar calculation and links the transition leaf. The display and enum probes leave that leaf out of their closure. On venus with LDC 1.43.0 the rule creation probe adds 15,580 O3 and 12,124 Oz incremental loadable bytes with 579 DMD template instances. Display adds 13,444 and 9,612 bytes with 552 instances. Time definition enumeration and name lookup add 7,188 and 5,700 bytes with 515 instances. The unit tests check zero GC bytes to construct, apply and hash a valid rule. The existing transition probes retain their measured sizes.
- **Instantiate typed lists only for fixed zone rules callers that request them.** Offset, daylight-savings, validity and transition-absence queries allocate zero GC bytes. Separate probes check the scalar queries and the typed collection views. On venus with LDC 1.43.0 the fixed queries and the transition lookups each add 1,988 O3 and 812 Oz incremental loadable bytes with 599 and 645 DMD instances respectively. Display adds 5,364 and 2,916 bytes with 475 instances. The valid-offset singleton adds 49,080 and 43,320 bytes with 876 instances. Requesting both historic and recurring transition views adds 94,412 and 83,692 bytes with 1,346 instances, including the existing typed empty-list implementations. The old probes retain their measured sizes.
- **Link supplied transition storage through its factory.** The input lists become immutable snapshots. Scalar historical queries binary-search those snapshots, and a future query expands up to 16 recurring rules in a stack buffer. Tests on both compilers verify zero GC bytes on valid historical and future queries. On venus with LDC 1.43.0, the supplied factory and scalar probe add 125,604 O3 and 110,692 Oz incremental loadable bytes and 1,673 DMD template instances, including its typed input collections. Fixed scalar queries add 2,388 and 1,244 bytes with 719 instances. Fixed transition lookup adds 2,060 and 884 bytes with 748 instances. Fixed display adds 5,404 and 2,956 with 594 instances. The fixed singleton and transition list probe sizes hold at 49,080/43,320 and 94,412/83,692, with 998 and 1,454 templates respectively. None of the fixed probes retains the supplied storage or recurring transition code.

- **Treat null calendar parse slices as empty under R26.** Period, Year, YearMonth and MonthDay share their existing empty-input diagnostic for every zero-length text slice. On venus with stock LDC 1.43.0 and DMD against base `cb82fade1`, the java-time module probe fell from 23,084 to 22,252 incremental loadable bytes and from 693 to 689 template instances. Each affected parser-and-renderer closure declined by 816 to 848 bytes across O3 and Oz. The other 42 closure results retained the same byte counts.

- **Use the empty-name path for null TimeDefinition slices under R26.** Dropping the dedicated null overload and branch reduced the enum link probe by 920 incremental loadable bytes in each of the stock LDC 1.43.0 O3 and Oz profiles against `6db8c28c6` on venus. The other 48 closure results and the java-time module's 22,252 incremental bytes stayed unchanged. The module's DMD template count fell from 689 to 687.
- **Check epoch milliseconds with both seconds and the fractional part.** `Date.from` accepts the first partial second within the signed millisecond range and rejects overflow with the Java cause chain. The Java 21 witness and both D compiler tests cover the endpoints and the fractional boundaries. The stock O3/Oz conversion probe against `18199b85` adds 444/460 loadable bytes and retains the same Java module records. The extra exception allocation occurs only when conversion overflows.
- **DMD folds `x - x == 0` to true, so it cannot test whether a value is finite.** DMD 2.114 rewrites `x - x` as `0` for floating-point operands at every optimisation level, even without `-O`. A finiteness check written that way therefore returns true for NaN and infinity. LDC 1.43.0 evaluates it correctly, so a test that runs only on LDC misses the fault. Test the exponent field of the raw bits instead: `(doubleToRawLongBits(x) & DOUBLE_EXP_BIT_MASK) != DOUBLE_EXP_BIT_MASK`, or the `float` version with `floatToRawIntBits` and `FLOAT_EXP_BIT_MASK`. `Math.fma` (`d992a2a54`) screens its operands this way. Its 3,323,328-call raw-bit oracle against Java 21 matched on DMD and LDC in debug and release builds.

## 6. Checklist for porting a module

Before a ported module lands:

1. **Allocations.** Count GC allocations per call on its hot paths with the counting method in gc.md. Only user-visible objects may remain (R-1 to R-8). Add or lower its entry in `policy/request-allocations.json` once the gate exists.
2. **Hot-path engine code** is `nothrow @nogc`, returns `Result`, and uses the arena or reused buffers.
3. **No per-call parsing** of anything that could be parsed once and cached (R-2); no monitors on lookups (R-7).
4. **Templates.** Generic public APIs are thin façades over erased engines (B-1). No heavyweight Phobos templates (B-2). The module stays within its template budget.
5. **Size.** Linked bytes stay within the module's ratio and ceiling. Optional functionality is unreachable unless used (S-1). No `std.process`, `std.file` or `std.path` (S-2).

   The scalar atomic leaves use druntime's ordered atomics and keep the legacy imports as re-exports. On venus against `c06ba54f` with LDC 1.43.0, the `java` size probe measured 132,232 incremental loadable bytes at O3 and 125,320 at Oz before and after the split. DMD template instances rose from 6,287 to 6,394 within the 7,350 cap. The three [scalar link-closure probes](../../framework/java/closure/README.md) had zero findings at O3 and Oz.

   The `URI.create` closure probe on venus with LDC 1.43.0 grew from 6,220 to 10,444 incremental loadable bytes at O3 and from 4,716 to 7,908 at Oz against `0208210db`. The existing character scan dispatches to the percent validator only when it finds a percent marker. The validator checks ASCII triplets and shares the IPv6 literal parser with the URL port. It uses borrowed slices. Both profiles retained their declared link closure.

   The `URI.create` message follow-up measures UTF-16 indices with the shared text engine only on the error path. Against `415dbdc38`, URI incremental loadable bytes went from 10,444 to 13,820 at O3 and 7,908 to 10,172 at Oz under the same venus probe. Both profiles passed the link-closure audit.

6. **Exceptions** used for control flow skip backtraces (R-11).
7. **Shared state.** Anything created once and shared is immutable after warm-up, or protected, and has a concurrent test (R-4).
8. **Generated code.** Anything the module asks Flint AOT to generate instantiates nothing per type it can look up (B-3).
9. **Records.** New findings are added to this file in the same commit.

| R-205 | **A repeating Java task reschedules itself through a host hook; the managed worker stays one-shot, and the periodic future owns its next deadline.** `ScheduledExecutorService.scheduleAtFixedRate` and `scheduleWithFixedDelay` build a `PeriodicTask` over the same `ManagedBackground` worker as `schedule`. After a successful run the task asks its `PeriodicHost` (the concrete service) for the next deadline; the host computes the fixed-rate start-to-start or fixed-delay run-to-run time, publishes it with `setDue`, and re-inserts the task while the service is open. Stopping refuses the re-insertion and cancels the task, so `shutdown` (Java's default `continueExistingPeriodicTasksAfterShutdown` is false) cancels periodic tasks while one-shot deadlines still drain. The periodic future completes only by cancellation (`CancellationException`) or by the task's exception (`ExecutionException`), and `isDone` is false between runs, as `FutureTask.runAndReset` leaves it. The service never downcasts a `Runnable` to its private interface on the hot path; the hook takes the periodic interface directly, which keeps the periodic module out of the plain `executorservice` probe's archive object. | The provider-equivalent control implements the same periodic future directly on the existing `future`, `delayed` and `scheduledfuture` leaves. On the same base the leaf probe measured 15,444 O3 / 13,856 Oz incremental loadable bytes and 1,000 joint DMD template instances against the unchanged caps of 16,800 bytes and 1,085 templates; the service leaf stayed at 54,124 O3 / 49,164 Oz bytes and 813 templates against 59,537 and 868, and the executorservice probe's archive object holds no periodic symbol. | `framework/java-concurrent-service/tests/scheduled_executor_service_test.d` (repetition, cancel, failure, timed get, shutdown cancellation, argument validation, `isPeriodic`), the closure probe `closure/scheduledexecutorservice.d` with its provider control, `leaf-budgets.json`, and the `java-concurrent-service` definition-of-done `link-closure` and `budgets` steps. |
